| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000170.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | The CX Agent Studio service agent `service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ces.iam.gserviceaccount.com` **MUST** be granted only the minimum IAM roles required by the approved tools in that project.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Rationale                    | Google documents that OpenAPI tool actions execute with the permissions granted to the CX Agent Studio service account rather than those of the end user, and names minimum IAM on that account as one of its three recommended mitigations. Every role granted to the service agent is therefore added to the effective reach of every agent in the project, including agents a different team built and agents reachable by anonymous callers. |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Notes                        | Treat each new grant to this service agent as a change to the blast radius of every agent in the project, and require the same review as adding a tool. Where a tool needs broad access to a backend, the correct answer is usually a separate project rather than a broader grant. Google's other two stated mitigations, dedicated projects and VPC Service Controls, are each carried by their own requirement in this guide.                 |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Mappings                     | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.308(a)(4) (Information Access Management); NIST 800-53: AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), IA-9 (Service Identification and Authentication), CM-7 (Least Functionality); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI03 (Identity and Privilege Abuse)                                                                                                |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000180.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Service account keys **MUST NOT** be created for any identity holding a Gemini Enterprise for CX role. |
| Rationale | A downloaded service account key is a long-lived bearer credential that bypasses federation, conditional access, and multi-factor enforcement, and it does not expire on personnel departure. Because the client role is a conversation-initiation credential and the editor roles alter what agents can reach, a leaked key for either is directly equivalent to unauthorized access to member conversation content. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Use workload identity federation for external callers and attached service accounts for workloads inside Google Cloud. The self-hosted token broker pattern runs on Cloud Run functions with a service account that needs no key. Enforce with the organization policy constraint `iam.managed.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation` (or the legacy `iam.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation`), which disables the creation of new external service account keys. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://cloud.google.com/iam/docs/best-practices-for-managing-service-account-keys; https://cloud.google.com/resource-manager/docs/organization-policy/restricting-service-accounts |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication); NIST 800-53: IA-5 (Authenticator Management), IA-9 (Service Identification and Authentication), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.q (User Identification and Authentication) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000190.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Entitlements to all `ces.googleapis.com` and `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` roles **MUST** be recertified at least quarterly by the accountable business owner. |
| Rationale | Access to this suite accumulates through pilots, incident response, and vendor-led enablement sessions, and none of those paths has a natural expiry. Because CX Insights project-level roles expose every conversation in the project, fine-grained scoping being Pre-GA and therefore unavailable as the control of record, an unreviewed viewer grant is standing access to the complete member conversation corpus rather than to a bounded slice of it. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Recertify the CX Insights viewer and editor populations with particular care, and treat the approved CX Insights access group as the authoritative list. Recertification should confirm both that the person still needs access and that the role held is still the narrowest one that fits the job. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(3) (Workforce Security), 164.308(a)(4) (Information Access Management); NIST 800-53: AC-6(7) (Review of User Privileges), AC-2 (Account Management), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.e (Review of User Access Rights) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000200.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| :--------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | Contact center representatives receiving Agent Assist suggestions **MUST** authenticate through Highmark's identity provider (IDP); Entra ID.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Rationale                    | Agent Assist places generated summaries, suggested replies, and knowledge excerpts in front of a human handling a live member interaction, and CX Insights subsequently evaluates that human's performance against the same conversation record. Both the disclosure of conversation content to a representative and the attribution of a quality assessment to one depend on knowing reliably which representative was present.                                                                                                                                |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Notes                        | The agent identifier is also the field CX Insights authorized views filter on, so a reliable representative identity is a prerequisite for any future adoption of fine-grained access control once it leaves Preview. Confirm the identifier propagated by the contact centre platform is the enterprise identity and not a platform-local seat number. The authenticated identity must also be recorded against each conversation the representative handles, since attribution is what makes both the disclosure and any later quality assessment defensible. |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://cloud.google.com/iam/docs/workforce-identity-federation; https://cloud.google.com/architecture/identity/single-sign-on                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Mappings                     | HIPAA: 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication), 164.312(b) (Audit Controls); NIST 800-53: IA-2 (Identification and Authentication (Organizational Users)), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records), AC-3 (Access Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.q (User Identification and Authentication), 09.aa (Audit Logging)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000210.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| :--------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | Standing administrative access to production Gemini Enterprise for CX projects **SHOULD** be replaced by time-bound, approved elevation. i.e., managed by PIM.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Rationale                    | The administrative actions this suite requires - changing a guardrail, registering a tool, altering retention - are infrequent and individually short. Standing admin rights convert an occasional need into a permanent exposure, and because several of those actions are irreversible in effect, an attacker holding a compromised standing credential does not need to wait for an opportunity. |
| Estimated Security Value     | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Notes                        | Elevation requests should name the change being made so the audit log entry can be correlated to an approval. This pairs with administrative-action alerting: an action with no corresponding elevation record is the signal worth investigating.                                                                                                                                                   |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Mappings                     | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(4) (Information Access Management); NIST 800-53: AC-2(11) (Usage Conditions), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-6(1) (Authorize Access to Security Functions), AU-6 (Audit Record Review, Analysis, and Reporting), AC-2 (Account Management); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management)                                                                                              |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000220.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| :--------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | Access to Gemini Enterprise for CX administrative surfaces **SHOULD** be conditioned on managed-device posture.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Rationale                    | Builder and administrator sessions display member conversation content and permit configuration changes that alter what production agents can reach. An unmanaged endpoint provides no assurance of disk encryption, endpoint detection, or absence of credential-stealing malware, so a session from one extends the trust boundary to a device the organization cannot attest. |
| Estimated Security Value     | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Notes                        | Implement at the identity provider or through Context-Aware Access rather than inside the product, which exposes no device-posture control of its own. Apply at minimum to the admin, securitySettingsEditor, deploymentEditor, and CX Insights viewer populations.                                                                                                              |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001410.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The `roles/ces.appEditor` role **SHOULD** be held by a different set of individuals than those holding `roles/ces.agentEditor` or `roles/ces.toolsEditor` in projects serving production traffic. |
| Rationale | Redaction, Cloud Logging, BigQuery export, audio recording, and interaction data storage are agent application settings, changed with the `ces.apps.update` permission that the App Editor role holds and the agent and tool editor roles do not. Where one person holds both sides, the same identity can change what an agent reaches and switch off the redaction or the record that would show what it did, with no second party in the path. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  App Editor also creates and imports applications and creates and restores application versions, so separating it moves those steps to a release or platform role; that operational cost is why this is a recommendation rather than a hard requirement. Where a team is too small to separate the roles, record the exception and rely on alerts for application setting changes, correlated with an approved change record, as the compensating control. `roles/ces.admin` holds both sides and is governed by the admin role's own restrictions. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions/ces; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(3) (Workforce Security), 164.312(b) (Audit Controls); NIST 800-53: AC-5 (Separation of Duties), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management), 09.ac (Protection of Log Information) |

## Agents & Agent Management

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000230.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| :--------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Requirement                  | Guardrails **MUST** be explicitly configured and documented on every agent application rather than left at the shipped default.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Rationale                    | Google states that each agent application is provided with default guardrails that the builder may modify, but the documentation does not name which safety level or which protections that default comprises. A control whose configured value is unknown cannot be attested to an assessor, cannot be drift-checked, and may change when Google revises the default for new applications. Explicit configuration converts an unknown into a baseline. |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Notes                        | Record the four guardrail dimensions per agent: Prompt guard state and outcome, Blocklist contents and match mode, Safety level, and any Rules. This inventory is the artifact the quarterly configuration drift check compares against. Confirm the configured values by reading them back through the API rather than trusting the console form state.                                                                                                |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Mappings                     | NIST 800-53: CM-6 (Configuration Settings), CM-2 (Baseline Configuration), SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), SI-10 (Information Input Validation); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM01 (Prompt Injection)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Detective Policies           |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Sentinel Modules             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000240.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| :--------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | The Safety guardrail **MUST** be set to Strict on every member-facing agent application, and to Strict or Balanced on every other agent application that can reach member data.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Rationale                    | Google describes Relaxed as prioritizing flexible generation and low latency, which is an explicit trade of harm filtering for responsiveness. On a member-facing health or benefits conversation, the harms the filter addresses are precisely the ones with regulatory and reputational consequence, and latency is not the constraint worth optimizing against them.                                           |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Notes                        | Strict prioritizes deep harm filtering and is required for member-facing agents; Balanced is permitted for internal-facing agents where a documented rationale exists. Record which level each agent uses and why, because the choice is invisible in the conversation transcript and cannot be reconstructed later. The Relaxed setting is not acceptable in production on any agent that can reach member data. |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Mappings                     | NIST 800-53: SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), SI-10 (Information Input Validation), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM09 (Misinformation); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0054 (LLM Jailbreak)                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000250.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| :--------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | Prompt guard **MUST** be enabled on every agent application, with a defined outcome for a triggered guard.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Rationale                    | Prompt guard is the product's stated protection against prompt-based attacks such as instructing the agent to ignore its instructions. In this suite the injected instruction does not have to come from the member: tool responses, retrieved data store content, and knowledge base excerpts all enter the model context, so an attacker who can influence any of those sources reaches the same objective indirectly.                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Notes                        | Choosing the outcome matters as much as enabling the guard. "Say exactly" is a safe terminal state. The "Handoff to an agent" outcome transfers control to another CX Agent Studio agent, not to a person; to reach a human, hand off to an agent whose only behavior is to call the `end_session` system tool with `session_escalated` set to True. A generated response re-enters the model with the adversarial content still in context. Prompt guard is a filter and not a boundary; the architectural half of this control is treating tool output and retrieved content as untrusted input. |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/system                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Mappings                     | NIST 800-53: SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SI-15 (Information Output Filtering); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM01 (Prompt Injection); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0051.001 (LLM Prompt Injection - Indirect)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Detective Policies           |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Sentinel Modules             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| CSP Level Controls           |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Monitoring Controls          |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000260.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A Blocklist guardrail **SHOULD** be configured on member-facing agent applications to suppress terms the organization has designated as prohibited in a generated response. |
| Rationale | Safety filters address broad harm categories and will not catch organization-specific disclosures: internal system names, unreleased product terms, legally constrained phrasing around coverage determinations, or competitor references. The Blocklist is the only term-matching guardrail, and it applies to agent responses as well as user input. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Apply the Blocklist to agent responses at minimum; applying it to user input as well is useful for detecting probing but will generate false positives on legitimate member language. The regex match mode is powerful and correspondingly easy to write too broadly - test against a sample of real transcripts before deployment. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SI-10 (Information Input Validation); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM02 (Sensitive Information Disclosure) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000270.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent instructions, prompts, application variables, and tool configurations **MUST NOT** contain credentials, API keys, tokens, or connection strings. |
| Rationale | Agent instructions and application variables are documented as data at rest inside CX Agent Studio and are readable by every holder of the viewer role, exportable through the import and export path, and reachable by the model itself at inference time. A secret placed there is therefore disclosed to a wider population than any secret store would permit and can be surfaced verbatim in a generated response through prompt injection. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Google supports Secret Manager for OpenAPI tool credentials, and MCP tools carry the same authentication options, so there is a supported path for every case where a secret is genuinely needed. Scan agent instructions and variables for high-entropy strings and known key formats as part of the pre-deployment agent security review. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/region; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication); NIST 800-53: IA-5 (Authenticator Management), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 10.g (Key Management); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM02 (Sensitive Information Disclosure) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000280.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Requirement                  | Every agent application **MUST** go through proper security review (SIA), etc.and approved before it is deployed to a channel carrying production traffic.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Rationale                    | The security posture of an agent is the union of its instructions, its guardrails, its tool set, and the reach of the project service agent, and no single console screen shows that union. Because tools execute with the platform identity, an agent that looks harmless in isolation can inherit the full backend reach of every other tool in its project, which only a review that considers the project as a whole will catch. |
| Estimated Security Value     | High                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Notes                        | The review covers at minimum: the tool list and what each tool can reach, guardrail configuration, whether any data store grounds the agent on content narrower in audience than the agent itself, the deployment channel and whether it permits anonymous access, and whether the agent handles determination-bearing content that requires a human decision maker. Record the approval as certification evidence.                  |
| References                   | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| ID                           | SG-GCP-GECX-000290.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| :--------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Requirement                  | Agent evaluations **MUST** be executed and reviewed against a documented test set before an agent application is promoted to production, and after any change to its instructions, tools, or guardrails.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Rationale                    | CX Agent Studio provides an evaluation capability with import and export of evaluation data, which makes a regression test set a supported artifact rather than an aspiration. Agent behavior is emergent rather than specified, so a change to instructions intended to improve one interaction can silently alter refusal behaviour, tool selection, or disclosure in another, and only a held test set will detect it. |
| Estimated Security Value     | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Audit Procedures             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Notes                        | The test set must include adversarial cases, not only happy paths: prompt injection attempts, requests for information the agent should refuse, and attempts to induce a tool call the caller is not entitled to. Evaluation operations may appear on the v1beta API surface, which this guide restricts to non-production projects, so run evaluations there and promote only the reviewed configuration.                |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000300.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent applications **MUST NOT** be the sole authority for a coverage, eligibility, clinical, or other adverse determination communicated to a member. |
| Rationale | A generative agent produces a fluent answer whether or not the underlying retrieval succeeded. The data store tool's grounding score setting can suppress weakly grounded responses, but nothing in the suite reliably prevents an ungrounded or incorrect determination from being delivered. An adverse determination carries notice, appeal, and accuracy obligations under claims-procedure rules such as 29 CFR 2560.503-1, which governs ERISA-covered employee benefit plans, and those obligations attach to the organization regardless of which system produced the text. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Constrain member-facing agents to informational responses and route determination-bearing interactions to a human with the agent acting as an assistant. Where an agent surfaces a determination made elsewhere, it must present the determination reached by the system of record rather than restating it in generated language. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/data-store; https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-29/subtitle-B/chapter-XXV/subchapter-G/part-2560/section-2560.503-1 |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: SA-8 (Security and Privacy Engineering Principles), PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM09 (Misinformation); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI09 (Human-Agent Trust Exploitation); NIST AI RMF (AI 100-1): GOVERN, MANAGE |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000310.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Members interacting with a Gemini Enterprise for CX agent **MUST** be informed that they are interacting with an automated agent and offered a path to reach a human. |
| Rationale | Members are entitled to know when they are dealing with an automated agent, and some state laws restrict undisclosed bot communication online - for example, California Business and Professions Code section 17941 in commercial and electoral contexts. A member who cannot reach a person has no way to correct an agent that has misunderstood a health or benefits question. The suite supports escalation: Google documents the `end_session` system tool as used "to escalate to a human agent", and Agent Assist exists to support the human who receives the escalation, so the path is available and needs only to be configured and disclosed. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Configure the fallback behavior and the guardrail outcomes so that escalation reaches a person. The "Handoff to an agent" outcome transfers control to another CX Agent Studio agent, not a human, so route it to an agent that calls `end_session` with `session_escalated` set to True, and confirm the human receiving the handoff has the conversation context. Confirm with counsel which state disclosure laws apply to each channel. Verify the disclosure appears on every approved deployment channel, including voice, where it must be spoken rather than displayed. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/system |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: PT-5 (Privacy Notice), PT-5(1) (Just-in-time Notice); NIST AI RMF (AI 100-1): GOVERN, MANAGE |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000320.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent applications generated by the Start with AI capability **MUST** be reviewed in full, including their generated instructions and tools, before deployment. |
| Rationale | Start with AI generates a starter agent and its tools from supplied documentation, which means both the agent's instructions and its tool registrations are model output rather than a human design. The generated tool set in particular determines what the agent can reach, and it is produced by inference over documentation rather than by an authorization decision. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Treat the generated artifact as a draft. The standard pre-deployment agent security review applies unchanged, with additional attention to tools the generator created that no one requested. Start with AI is itself Preview: its feature page carries the Pre-GA Offerings Terms as of 2026-09-24, and the February 4, 2026 release note declares CX Agent Studio generally available and lists Start with AI among the launch's capabilities without stating that the feature is GA. Use it only in a non-production project, on documentation that contains no protected health information or other personal data, and review its output here before any of it reaches production. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/quick/generate-agent; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), AC-6 (Least Privilege), CM-4 (Impact Analyses) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000330.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Where one agent is invoked as a tool of another - within one agent application or, through the A2A Protocol, across agent applications and projects - the delegation chain **MUST** be documented and reviewed as a single authorization surface. |
| Rationale | Google's release notes announced agents as tools on April 17, 2026 and declared the capability generally available on September 24, 2026. An agent-as-a-tool is another agent in the same application, invoked with its own instructions and tool set. Because those tools execute with the identities configured on them - the project service agent where Service agent ID token authentication is used - rather than the end user's, a delegation chain does not narrow authority at each hop: the invoked agent's tools reach what their identities reach, regardless of what the caller was entitled to. A chain therefore aggregates rather than attenuates reach, and reviewing either agent alone will not reveal it. The A2A Protocol extends the same chain across application and project boundaries: a remote CX Agent Studio application is reached through the calling project's service agent, and an inbound A2A caller can write agent context directly into the invoked session. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Record the full graph of which agents invoke which, and review the union of tools reachable through the graph rather than the tool list of any single agent. A cycle or a chain crossing a trust boundary is a finding. For each A2A link also record the authentication method, the identity each side presents, what session context crosses, and which project owns each end; the A2A Protocol tool is Preview as of 2026-09-24. This is the strongest single argument for separating projects by trust domain. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/agent-as-tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/a2a-protocol |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management), 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM06 (Excessive Agency); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI07 (Insecure Inter-Agent Communication); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0053 (AI Agent Tool Invocation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000340.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent versioning and changelog history **SHOULD** be retained for the life of the agent application. |
| Rationale | CX Agent Studio provides granular changelogs and rollback as a product capability. That history is the only record of what an agent was instructed to do at a given moment, which is what an incident investigation, a member complaint, or a regulatory inquiry will ask about. Conversation transcripts alone do not answer why the agent said what it said. |
| Estimated Security Value | Low |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where an investigation may need to reconstruct behavior beyond the retained changelog window, export the agent configuration on a schedule and retain the exports under the organization's records retention policy. Rollback is also the fastest containment action for a bad agent change, so exercise it at least once before relying on it in an incident. Do not prune changelog history as part of routine housekeeping. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls); NIST 800-53: CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), AU-11 (Audit Record Retention), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), CP-9 (System Backup); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

## AI Models & Model Management

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001260.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Only models on the organization's approved model allowlist **MUST** be selected to power a Gemini Enterprise for CX agent application. |
| Rationale | CX Agent Studio exposes a model picker offering several Gemini variants, and the choice determines the reasoning quality, the safety behaviour, and the latency of every member conversation the agent handles. Google states that CX Agent Studio models are performance-tuned for customer experience and may differ from the standard Vertex AI offering of the same name, so a model's behaviour here cannot be inferred from testing it elsewhere. Without an allowlist the model is whatever the builder picked from a dropdown, and it changes silently whenever Google revises the list. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Maintain the allowlist as an explicit artifact naming each approved model and the date its launch stage was checked, and reconcile it against the picker at the monthly release-note review. Voice-optimized and text-optimized models are separate entries and need separate approval, because a voice deployment reaches a different model than the text deployment of the same agent. Google can also change the model without builder action: the May 26, 2026 release note states that users who had not manually updated were automatically upgraded from Gemini 2.0 to Gemini 2.5 models. Treat such forced upgrades as allowlist changes and re-run the agent evaluations. The composite-v1 voice model, generally available since 2026-09-24, combines listener, thinking, and speaker sub-models whose minor versions Google may update automatically; approve it as one entry and re-run the voice agent evaluations at each monthly review rather than only on a visible model change. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/agent; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: CM-6 (Configuration Settings), CM-7 (Least Functionality), CM-2 (Baseline Configuration), SA-4 (Acquisition Process); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM03 (Supply Chain); NIST AI RMF (AI 100-1): GOVERN, MAP |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001270.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A model designated Preview in the CX Agent Studio model picker **MUST NOT** be selected for an agent application carrying production traffic. |
| Rationale | Google marks at least one model in the picker as Preview while presenting it alongside generally available models in the same dropdown, with no separation in the interface. A Preview model is governed by the Pre-GA Offerings Terms: no SLA, changeable or withdrawable without notice, and subject to Google's statement that Pre-GA offerings should not be used "to process personal data or other data subject to legal or regulatory compliance requirements". Google's HIPAA guidance says separately not to use Pre-GA offerings in connection with PHI. A builder selecting it is making a contractual and compliance decision through a dropdown that gives no indication one was made. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  As of 2026-09-24 the Preview entry was gemini-3-flash, with composite-v1, gemini-3.7-flash, gemini-3.5-flash, gemini-2.5-flash and gemini-3.1-flash-live shown as generally available. Verify the current designations in the picker rather than relying on that list, because the composition changes without a release note. Enforce by reviewing the selected model during the pre-deployment agent security review. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/agent; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(8) (Evaluation); NIST 800-53: CM-7 (Least Functionality), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), SA-4 (Acquisition Process), SA-8 (Security and Privacy Engineering Principles) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001280.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A model override applied to an individual sub-agent **MUST** be documented and approved as a change to the agent application. |
| Rationale | Google states that a global model can be set for the agent application and overridden for specific sub-agents. An override is invisible from the application-level configuration, so an agent reviewed and approved on one model can contain a sub-agent running on another, including one that is Preview or not on the allowlist. Because sub-agents frequently handle the narrower and more sensitive parts of a conversation, the override lands precisely where it matters most. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enumerate sub-agent model assignments explicitly during review rather than reading the application-level setting alone, and record the effective model per sub-agent in the configuration baseline so the quarterly drift check can detect a change. Google states that audio agent applications do not allow overriding an agent's model except when the agent is used as a tool, so in voice applications check the model set on each agent used as a tool. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/agent; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/agent-as-tool |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), CM-2 (Baseline Configuration), CM-4 (Impact Analyses); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM03 (Supply Chain) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001290.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The launch stage of a selected model **MUST** be verified in the CX Agent Studio interface rather than inferred from that model's status in other Google Cloud products. |
| Rationale | Google states that all models in CX Agent Studio are considered generally available unless explicitly marked Preview in the interface, and that this launch stage is specific to CX Agent Studio and operates independently of a model's status in other Google Cloud products. A model that is generally available in Vertex AI may therefore be Preview here, and the reverse also holds. An engineer checking the model's status on its Vertex AI documentation page will reach a conclusion that does not govern this product. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  This is the reason the allowlist records where and when each launch stage was checked. Treat the CX Agent Studio picker as the only authoritative source for a model's stage within this product, and re-verify at each monthly release-note review rather than at model selection alone. |

## Tools & External Integrations

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000350.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Each Gemini Enterprise for CX project **MUST** contain only agent applications that share a single trust domain. |
| Rationale | Google states that OpenAPI tool actions execute with the permissions granted to the CX Agent Studio service account rather than the end user's, and that service agent is a single identity per project. Every IAM grant made to it is therefore available to every agent application in the project whose tools authenticate as the service agent, and no in-product setting narrows those grants per application; tools configured with their own service account, OAuth, or API key credentials add their own reach on top. Combined with the Google-hosted token broker's public access path, the worst plausible configuration is an unauthenticated caller whose reach includes every grant held by the project service agent. For the service agent's permissions, the project boundary is the only boundary this product offers. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | High |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  This decision is made before the first agent application exists and is expensive to reverse, because moving an agent means recreating it, its tools, its deployment channels, and its data stores in a new project with a new perimeter. Google names dedicated projects as its own first recommended mitigation. Where a shared project is proposed for cost or operational reasons, the residual risk to accept is explicitly that every agent in it inherits every grant made to the project service agent. Agents serving different audiences or reaching different sensitivity tiers belong in separate projects, because the project is the scope of the service agent whose permissions every tool executes with. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.308(a)(4) (Information Access Management); NIST 800-53: AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI03 (Identity and Privilege Abuse); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0053 (AI Agent Tool Invocation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000360.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | An inventory of every registered tool, its type, its target system, its authentication method, and its approving owner **MUST** be maintained and reviewed at least quarterly. |
| Rationale | CX Agent Studio offers a growing catalog of tool types - eighteen in the tools overview as of 2026-09-24 - spanning arbitrary HTTP APIs, MCP servers, SaaS connectors, data stores, RAG knowledge bases, client-side functions, and code execution, and any holder of the toolsEditor role can register one without a deployment. Because a registered tool extends what agents in the project can reach - an agent reaches it once assigned, and a Python code tool can call any tool defined in its application without assignment - an unmaintained inventory means the project's effective reach is unknown to the people accountable for it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The inventory is the input to the project-boundary judgement, so it must record what each tool can reach rather than only what it is called. Tool registration and modification (CreateTool, UpdateTool, and DeleteTool are DATA_WRITE methods) appear in Data Access audit logs under `ces.googleapis.com` and should be alerted on so the inventory can be reconciled against actual changes; confirm Data Access audit logging is enabled for the service, because the reconciliation depends on it. Record assignment as well as registration: which agents each tool is assigned to, and whether the application contains a Python code tool. A2A Protocol tools, added to the tools overview on 2026-09-24 as Preview, reach remote agents; record the remote agent endpoint, its owner, and its authentication method if one appears in a project, as well as any remote agent tool type found in the REST tool resource. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/a2a-protocol |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: CM-8 (System Component Inventory), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), AC-6 (Least Privilege), SA-9 (External System Services) |
