| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000370.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Static credentials used by OpenAPI and MCP tools - API keys and any other secret held in the tool configuration - **MUST** be stored in Secret Manager and referenced rather than embedded in tool configuration, agent instructions, or custom header values. |
| Rationale | Google documents Secret Manager as the supported credential store for OpenAPI tools, with Secret Manager Secret Accessor granted to the CX Agent Studio service agent, and states that MCP tools carry the same authentication options. A credential held anywhere else in the product is readable by every holder of the viewer role, travels in configuration exports, and has no rotation or access-audit path of its own. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The Service agent ID token and Service Account authentication options are minted by the platform for each call and store no secret, so this requirement does not reach them; prefer them wherever the backend accepts a Google-issued identity. Google does not document where the OAuth option holds a client secret; confirm that before relying on it. MCP custom header values must be supplied by variables, which makes it tempting to place a static token in a variable - do not. Reference a Secret Manager secret instead. Note the perimeter interaction: Google documents that OpenAPI tools cannot reference authentication keys using secrets outside the VPC Service Controls perimeter, so the secret and the perimeter must be planned together. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication); NIST 800-53: IA-5 (Authenticator Management), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000380.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A tool **MUST NOT** be able to retrieve data beyond what the principal conversing with the agent is authorized to see, unless the backend authorizes every request against an identity bound to the session at authentication rather than supplied by the model. |
| Rationale | Because the tool executes as the project service agent and not as the caller, backend authorization is applied to the platform identity rather than to the member. A tool wired to an API that can return any member's record will return any member's record when the model is persuaded to ask for one, and no guardrail reliably prevents that because the request is a legitimate tool call. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The backend must bind each request to a verified identifier the agent cannot choose - a session-bound identifier established at authentication, not a value the model extracts from the conversation - and return only the objects that identifier is entitled to. A backend that cannot do that is not eligible for any agent whose audience is narrower than the backend's data, and an agent serving unauthenticated callers has no such identity, so its tools must not reach member data at all. This is the concrete form of the confused-deputy exposure that makes the project boundary the real security boundary in this product. CX Agent Studio passes no end-user identity to a tool backend on its own. For OpenAPI tools, the documented `x-ces-session-context` extension injects session context values, such as `$context.session_id`, into a request parameter or header without the model choosing them; use it, or an equivalent server-side binding, to carry the session-bound identifier. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API1 (Broken Object Level Authorization); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI02 (Tool Misuse and Exploitation); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0053 (AI Agent Tool Invocation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000390.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Data store tools and File search tools **MUST NOT** be grounded on content whose authorized audience is narrower than the audience able to converse with the agent. |
| Rationale | Neither the data store tool pages nor the REST definition of the data store tool offers any field or setting that carries the end user's identity into retrieval, whereas the connector tool in the same API explicitly supports end-user authentication. The same holds for File search tools, which retrieve from a RAG knowledge base. Absent a documented ACL-aware retrieval path, the safe reading is that any content ingested into a data store is reachable by any caller who can reach the agent, and the grounding score setting does not change who can see what. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Curate corpora to the agent's audience rather than relying on retrieval to filter: for a member-facing agent, public and member-general content only, with no internal policy documents, case notes, or provider-specific material. Connector data stores (for example Salesforce, Jira, Confluence, and BigQuery) are the type most likely to carry internal content and are within scope. Once a data store from one region is added to a tool, only data stores from that region can be added to it. The data store tool's Filter setting lets the model add a filter parameter to a retrieval call; the console lists Never as the default while the REST reference describes the unspecified default as including it for connector data stores, and in either case the filter is model-chosen and not an access control. This reading rests on the absence of a documented permission-aware retrieval mechanism rather than on a Google statement; confirm with Google before relying on per-user retrieval. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/data-store; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/cloud-storage-data-store; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/website-data-store; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/file |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), AC-21 (Information Sharing), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM02 (Sensitive Information Disclosure) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000400.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Content ingested into a data store or RAG knowledge base used by a member-facing agent **MUST** be reviewed and approved before each ingestion and ingested only from locations under the organization's control. |
| Rationale | Retrieved content enters the model's context and is acted upon as instruction-bearing input. Where a corpus includes externally authored material - a crawled website, a vendor document, a partner feed - an attacker who can edit that source has a durable indirect prompt injection path into every conversation the corpus grounds, without ever interacting with the agent. Ingesting only from locations the organization controls means the corpus changes only when someone the organization authorizes re-ingests it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Externally authored material, such as a vendor document or a partner publication, is admitted only as a reviewed snapshot copied into organization-controlled storage, and each refresh is a new ingestion that needs its own review. Website data stores are the highest-risk case because the crawl re-ingests whatever the page currently says, so restrict them to domains the organization controls - Google requires domain verification when website content is used as a source, which partly enforces this - and never point one at a third-party site. This requirement addresses the injection vector; the untrusted-input requirement addresses what the agent does with the result. File search tools, which upload files into a RAG knowledge base as a simpler alternative to data store tools, are in scope on the same terms. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/data-store; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/file; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/website-data-store |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SR-3 (Supply Chain Controls and Processes), CM-8 (System Component Inventory); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM01 (Prompt Injection); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI06 (Memory and Context Poisoning); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0051.001 (LLM Prompt Injection - Indirect) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000410.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Tool responses, retrieved data store content, and knowledge base excerpts **MUST** be treated as untrusted input that cannot authorize a subsequent action the caller could not authorize directly. |
| Rationale | Every source that enters the model context is a channel through which an instruction can arrive, and the model does not distinguish retrieved text from a member's utterance. An agent that will call a second tool because the first tool's response told it to has delegated its authorization decision to whoever controls that backend's response body, which in a multi-hop or third-party integration is not the organization. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enforce architecturally rather than through prompt engineering: constrain which tools an agent may call and in what sequence, and require any state-changing action to be gated on a condition established outside the model's context. The Prompt guard reduces the frequency of successful injection; it does not bound the consequence. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: SI-10 (Information Input Validation), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), SI-15 (Information Output Filtering); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM01 (Prompt Injection), LLM06 (Excessive Agency); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI01 (Agent Goal Hijack), ASI06 (Memory and Context Poisoning); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0051.001 (LLM Prompt Injection - Indirect), AML.T0053 (AI Agent Tool Invocation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000420.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Third-party and externally hosted MCP servers **MUST** pass a supply chain security review before being registered as a tool. |
| Rationale | Google documents that MCP tools may target prebuilt servers, servers built with third-party frameworks, or servers deployed by the customer, and that they carry the same authentication options as OpenAPI tools - meaning they may execute with the project service agent's permissions (Google recommends the Service Agent ID token for Cloud Run hosted servers) or with a service account, OAuth, or API key credential configured on the tool. An MCP server also declares its own tool definitions, so a compromised or malicious server controls both the description the model reasons over and the response it acts on. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The review covers who operates the server, how its tool definitions are versioned and change-controlled, what it logs, and whether its declared tools can change after registration. Only StreamableHttpTransport servers are supported; SSE transport servers are not, so a server offering only SSE cannot be used regardless of review outcome. An unreviewed public MCP server is never eligible for registration regardless of how convenient its tool set appears. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts), 164.308(a)(8) (Evaluation); NIST 800-53: SA-9 (External System Services), SR-3 (Supply Chain Controls and Processes), SR-11 (Component Authenticity), CM-7 (Least Functionality); HITRUST CSF v11: 05.i (Identification of Risks Related to External Parties), 05.k (Addressing Security in Third Party Agreements); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM03 (Supply Chain); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI04 (Agentic Supply Chain Vulnerabilities); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0110 (AI Agent Tool Poisoning) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000430.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | MCP servers used by production agent applications **SHOULD** be hosted within the organization's Google Cloud boundary, with invocation restricted to the CX Agent Studio service agent. |
| Rationale | Google's documented pattern hosts the MCP server on Cloud Run and grants the Cloud Run Invoker role to `service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ces.iam.gserviceaccount.com`. That arrangement keeps the tool definitions under the organization's change control, keeps conversation-derived parameters inside the boundary, and makes the server's own logs available for investigation - none of which holds for a server operated by a third party. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Restricting the Invoker role to the service agent alone also prevents the MCP server becoming an unauthenticated internal API. A VPC Service Controls perimeter narrows the network path but does not remove other IAM-authorized callers: any principal holding `run.routes.invoke` on the service or its project can still call it, so include the service's IAM policy and project-level Cloud Run grants in the review. Test the server independently with curl or Postman before registration, as Google recommends. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000440.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Tools that connect to Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira, Confluence, or SharePoint - both the dedicated tool types for those systems and Integration Connector tools - **MUST** authenticate with the end user's own credentials or with a dedicated connection identity scoped to the minimum records the agent requires. |
| Rationale | These systems hold case notes, ticket bodies, and document libraries whose contents routinely include member-identifying detail written by staff for staff. The dedicated Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira, Confluence, and SharePoint tools use the OpenAPI tool authentication options, and an Integration Connector tool passes the connection's admin authentication by default unless an end-user authentication override is configured. Unless end-user credentials are passed, the connection identity's entitlements become the agent's entitlements, and a connection created with an administrator's profile exposes the entire system through a conversational interface. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where the connection supports it, prefer the Integration Connectors authentication override, which replaces the default admin authentication with an end-user authentication config; the Connection must have authentication override enabled or tool creation fails. Otherwise create a purpose-built integration account in each SaaS platform rather than reusing an existing service or personal account, and scope it at the source system with object, field, and record filters. Re-verify scope after any change to the source system's permission model, which is outside Google Cloud's change control and will not surface in a CX Agent Studio review. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/connector; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/salesforce; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/service-now; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/jira; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/confluence; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/sharepoint; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary), 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts); NIST 800-53: AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SA-9 (External System Services), IA-9 (Service Identification and Authentication); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management), 05.k (Addressing Security in Third Party Agreements); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API1 (Broken Object Level Authorization) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000450.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Python code tools **MUST** be reviewed as application source code before registration and before any subsequent modification. |
| Rationale | A Python code tool is arbitrary executable logic invoked as part of a conversation, which places it in the same risk class as application code while offering none of the surrounding pipeline controls - no branch protection, no peer review gate, no dependency scanning, no build provenance. Google documents that the code runs in a sandbox on Python 3.12 with a fixed set of importable modules, that it can read the full conversation history and session state and can modify session state, that it can call any other tool defined in the agent application, and that it can make outbound requests only to endpoints publicly available on the internet, with no private network access even where Service Directory is configured. The code therefore holds the conversation, the reach of every tool in the application, and a public egress path at the same time. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Review each Python code tool for: every outbound request through the provided HTTP client, each one data leaving the organization over the public internet; every other tool it calls, since a call from code inherits that tool's authority (for OpenAPI tools, the service agent); every write to session state, since state set in code steers later turns; and unbounded input handling. The sandbox identity, and whether its HTTP client attaches a Google credential, is undocumented; confirm with Google before using this tool type with member data, and never place a secret in the code, which is stored in the tool configuration. Google's pages disagree twice (the tool page cites the requests library while the runtime reference uses the provided client; the REST resource carries a Service Directory field although the tool page says private network access is unavailable), so verify behaviour in a non-production project. A production modification without the same review is not permitted. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/python; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/python; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), SA-15 (Development Process, Standards, and Tools), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity), CM-7 (Least Functionality); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.s (Information Exchange Policies and Procedures); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM05 (Improper Output Handling); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI05 (Unexpected Code Execution) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000460.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Client function tools **MUST NOT** be passed data that the end user is not already authorized to see. |
| Rationale | Google states that client function tools are always executed on the client side, not by the agent: the model decides when to call the function and what its arguments contain, the arguments are handed to the calling client application, and the server-side session waits until the client returns a result. On the web widget or a mobile channel that client is the end user's own device, which is untrusted and on a public deployment may belong to an unauthenticated party. Data placed in a client function call is disclosed at the moment of the call regardless of what the function does with it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Define the parameters schema of each client function so that it can carry only fields the end user is already authorized to see, and review the function description, because the model uses it to decide when to call the function and what to pass. Treat the argument set as a disclosure to the end user in the data flow record. This requirement governs data sent to the client; results returned by the client are governed separately. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/function; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM02 (Sensitive Information Disclosure) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000470.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The Google Search tool **SHOULD NOT** be enabled on member-facing agent applications. |
| Rationale | Grounding a member conversation on open web results introduces content the organization neither authored nor controls into a channel members treat as authoritative, and it sends conversation-derived query terms outside the assessed boundary. For health and benefits questions the accuracy and provenance obligations sit with the organization no matter where the text originated. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where broad knowledge is genuinely needed, a curated and reviewed data store provides grounding the organization can review and version. If the Search tool is required for a specific internal-facing use case, document the query terms that can reach it and confirm they carry no member identifiers. Whether Google Search grounding is covered by the BAA was not confirmed at authoring; confirm with Google before any use that could carry member information, and treat the tool as prohibited on any agent that can reach member data if it is not covered. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/data-store |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI); NIST 800-53: AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration), SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SC-7 (Boundary Protection); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.s (Information Exchange Policies and Procedures); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM09 (Misinformation) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000480.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Where a tool's backend is reachable through Service Directory private network access, OpenAPI tools, MCP tools, and the dedicated Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira, Confluence, and SharePoint tools **MUST** be configured to use the Service Directory path rather than a public endpoint. |
| Rationale | Google documents Service Directory private network access as the supported way for CX Agent Studio to reach OpenAPI tool targets inside a VPC network, stating that this keeps traffic within the Google Cloud network and enforces IAM and VPC Service Controls. A tool that egresses to a public endpoint instead forfeits all three properties for every conversation parameter it carries. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Configure Service Directory private network access and grant `servicedirectory.viewer` and `servicedirectory.pscAuthorizedService` to `service-agent-project-number@gcp-sa-ces.iam.gserviceaccount.com`; where Service Directory lives in a different project, grant `servicedirectory.viewer` in both. Note that within a perimeter this stops being a preference - Google documents that tools and callbacks cannot send requests to arbitrary HTTP endpoints once VPC Service Controls is enabled without Service Directory configuration. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/salesforce; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/python |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), SC-8 (Transmission Confidentiality and Integrity); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls), 09.n (Security of Network Services) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000490.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The set of tools registered in a project **MUST** be restricted to those the approved agent use cases require, with unused registrations removed. |
| Rationale | Because tool authority aggregates at the project service agent rather than attenuating per agent, every registered tool that no agent needs is pure added blast radius. Tools also accumulate during development and vendor-led enablement sessions and are rarely removed, so the gap between what is registered and what is used widens silently over time. Registration extends reach even before a tool is assigned to an agent, because a Python code tool can call any tool defined in the same application. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Reconcile the tool inventory against actual tool invocation observed in conversation history and audit logs on the quarterly cycle. A tool registered but never invoked in a full quarter is a candidate for removal, and its continued presence should require a stated reason. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001310.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Tool invocations **MUST** carry a correlation identifier, comprising at minimum the agent application identifier and the conversation or session identifier, that the receiving backend system records in its own logs. |
| Rationale | Tool calls commonly reach the backend under a shared platform or tool identity - the CX Agent Studio service agent, or a service account, OAuth client, or API key configured on the tool - so the backend's own audit log records one identity for many agents, conversations, and members. Without a correlation identifier the backend cannot attribute an action to the conversation that caused it, which means an investigation starting from a backend record - a changed claim, an unexpected lookup, an anomalous query volume - has no path back into the conversation history. The Google Cloud side of the boundary is well instrumented; the identifier is what carries that attribution across it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  For OpenAPI tools, use the documented `x-ces-session-context` extension to inject session context such as `$context.session_id` into a header or parameter; for MCP tools, inject session context variables into custom headers; for Integration Connector tools, pass the identifier in a defined request field. Require the receiving system to log it as a condition of being connected. The identifier is metadata rather than content and must not carry member identifiers or conversation text. This is the control that makes the confused-deputy residual risk investigable after the fact rather than merely accepted. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/mcp; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(D) (Information System Activity Review); NIST 800-53: AU-3 (Content of Audit Records), AU-2 (Event Logging), IA-9 (Service Identification and Authentication), AC-3 (Access Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.aa (Audit Logging); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI03 (Identity and Privilege Abuse); MITRE ATLAS: AML.T0053 (AI Agent Tool Invocation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001330.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Results returned by a client function tool **MUST NOT** be relied upon to establish the end user's identity, entitlement, or authorization for any subsequent action. |
| Rationale | A client function tool is executed by the calling client application, which returns the result to the agent in the tool response and the agent then continues the conversation on it. On a web or mobile channel that client is under the end user's control, so the returned value is whatever the end user's device chooses to send. An agent that treats that value as proof of who the member is, or of what they may do, lets the member assert their own authorization. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Validate each result against the response schema declared on the function and treat it as untrusted input. Establish identity and entitlement from the channel's authentication and from backend systems reached by server-side tools, never from a client function result. Google's documentation describes the flow but does not itself characterize the result as untrusted; this requirement is the organization's reading of the documented flow. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/function; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.apps.tools |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: SI-10 (Information Input Validation), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), IA-8 (Identification and Authentication (Non-organizational Users)); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.q (User Identification and Authentication), 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API2 (Broken Authentication); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI03 (Identity and Privilege Abuse) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001340.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Data store tools that reference a search engine **MUST** name the specific data stores to be searched rather than leaving the data store selection empty. |
| Rationale | Google documents that when a data store tool targets an engine and its data store selection is empty, the search applies to all data stores associated with the engine. Any data store later attached to that engine for another purpose then becomes retrievable by every caller of the agent without any change to the agent or the tool, which silently widens the audience for that content. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001360.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The RAG knowledge base behind a File search tool **MUST** be located in a US region where RAG Engine is designated GA. |
| Rationale | A File search tool uploads files into a RAG knowledge base created in a location the builder selects, and RAG knowledge bases are a RAG Engine resource billed and regionalized separately from CX Agent Studio. The residency pin configured for CX Agent Studio therefore does not govern where these files are stored, and Google's RAG Engine region table lists US regions at different launch stages - us-central1 and us-east4 as Allowlist, GA and us-east1 as Allowlist, Preview. Choosing a US region fixes where the knowledge base is created; it is not a contractual data-residency commitment, which Google states RAG Engine does not support. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set the Location field explicitly when a local file is uploaded, because the knowledge base is created at that point. Confirm the region's launch stage in the RAG Engine region table at registration, because the table changes independently of CX Agent Studio release notes. Access to the US regions requires allowlisting by Google. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001390.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent-to-agent (A2A) Protocol tools **MUST NOT** be configured on any agent application, for outbound delegation or for inbound invocation, while the capability is designated Preview. |
| Rationale | The CX Agent Studio tools overview and documentation navigation mark the A2A Protocol tool Preview, although its own page carries no banner, so it is governed by the Pre-GA Offerings Terms and by Google's HIPAA guidance not to use Pre-GA offerings in connection with PHI. The capability also opens a boundary this guide otherwise keeps closed: external applications and orchestrators can invoke an agent application directly while holding the Client role, which includes `ces.tools.execute`, and can send agent context metadata that the application writes directly into the current session. Outbound, the tool delegates conversation-derived tasks to remote agents in other projects, in Vertex AI Agent Engine, or at third parties authenticated with an API key or OAuth. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  This is a specific application of the baseline prohibition on Pre-GA features, restated because the tool appears in the same picker as generally available tool types. Before approving it at GA, decide each direction separately. Inbound callers need a custom role without `ces.tools.execute` and a path through the organization's API gateway, and inbound session context must be treated as untrusted input. Outbound links need a documented remote owner, authentication method, and cross-project trust decision, and the delegation chain they create is reviewed as one authorization surface. Sessions with other CX Agent Studio applications are always stateful, so context persists across the link. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/a2a-protocol; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |

## Deployment & Channel Security

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000500.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | An inventory of every deployment channel, its authentication mode, and its approving owner **MUST** be maintained for each agent application. |
| Rationale | CX Agent Studio publishes an agent to a growing set of surfaces - as of 2026-09-24, web widget, AudioCodes, Five9, Google Cloud CCaaS, Google Telephony Platform, SecureCo, Twilio, WhatsApp and Instagram, and direct API access - and each carries a different authentication model and a different population of callers. The security posture of an agent is meaningless without knowing which of these it is exposed on, and a channel added later does not change anything visible in the agent's own configuration. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The inventory records, per channel, whether callers are authenticated, what identity the channel's token carries, and which third party operates the transport. Reconcile it against direct grants of the Gemini Enterprise for Customer Experience Client role (`roles/ces.client`) to service accounts and other non-human principals only. Every CX Agent Studio editor role and the Admin role inherit the Client role, and end users authenticated through OAuth2 hold access to the agent themselves, so a reconciliation against all holders will not balance. A direct non-human grant with no matching channel entry is either an undocumented channel or an unused credential. Inbound A2A callers, where the A2A Protocol is enabled, are channels for this inventory. Reconcile the channel list against the deployment overview at each release-note review rather than against a fixed count. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/secureco; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/whatsapp |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: CM-8 (System Component Inventory), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.j (User Authentication for External Connections), 01.v (Information Access Restriction) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000510.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Public access through the Google-hosted token broker **MUST NOT** be enabled for any agent application whose tools can reach member data. |
| Rationale | Google documents the Google-hosted token broker as the mechanism that allows public access to a chat widget, which means an unauthenticated caller obtains a valid token and converses with the agent. Because tools execute with the project service agent's permissions rather than the caller's, the effective reach of that anonymous conversation is every backend any tool in the project authenticates to. Anonymous access and service-agent tool execution are individually defensible and jointly a direct path from the open internet to member records. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where a genuinely public agent is required - a benefits FAQ or a store locator - place it in its own project with its own service agent and no tool that reaches member data, so that anonymous reach is bounded by the project rather than by the agent's instructions. Google recommends deploying agent applications in a dedicated project to limit the scope of permissions available to the CX Agent Studio service account. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication), 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), IA-8 (Identification and Authentication (Non-organizational Users)), AC-6 (Least Privilege), SC-7 (Boundary Protection); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.j (User Authentication for External Connections), 01.v (Information Access Restriction); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API2 (Broken Authentication), API1 (Broken Object Level Authorization) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000520.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Where public access is approved for a bounded public-information agent, origin checks and reCAPTCHA **MUST** both be enabled on the widget configuration. |
| Rationale | Google presents these as optional additions - the documentation reads enable public access, and optionally enable origin and reCAPTCHA checks, recommended to prevent spoofing and abuse. Left at their defaults they are off, which leaves a publicly reachable generative endpoint with no origin binding and no automation control: it can be embedded on any site, driven by a script, and consumed as a free inference endpoint at the organization's expense. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Origin checks bind the widget to the organization's own pages, which is what makes an embedded agent attributable. reCAPTCHA constrains automated abuse. Neither is a substitute for the reach limitation that comes from a dedicated project with no sensitive tools, and both must be verified after any widget configuration change because they sit in the same form as the public-access toggle. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: SC-5 (Denial-of-service Protection), SC-23 (Session Authenticity), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM10 (Unbounded Consumption); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API4 (Unrestricted Resource Consumption), API6 (Unrestricted Access to Sensitive Business Flows) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000530.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent applications serving authenticated members **MUST** authenticate each member individually through the organization's identity provider - using the OAuth2 client path or a custom authentication API that issues a short-lived token for the member's session - and grant member identities no role that includes the `ces.tools.execute` permission. |
| Rationale | The widget supports an OAuth2 client that authenticates the end user and returns a token operating on that user's behalf, and a custom authentication API that returns a Google access token or signed JWT permitted to call runSession. Google notes that the identity associated with the token - end user or service account - must hold the IAM permissions to interact with the agent. A service-account broker makes every member session indistinguishable at the platform layer, so the conversation record cannot be attributed and no per-member condition can be applied at the boundary. The predefined Client role grants tools.execute in addition to runSession and bidiRunSession, so a member identity holding it could invoke the agent's tools directly, outside the conversation, with the tools' configured identity. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where the OAuth2 path is used, grant member principals a custom role containing only `ces.sessions.runSession` (and `ces.sessions.bidiRunSession` for voice) rather than the predefined Client role. Google describes the custom option as an API that "returns a google access token or signed jwt that has the permission to call runSession in your app", which suits an organization-operated member identity provider. Authentication mode by agent type: agents serving authenticated members use per-member authentication; a self-hosted service-account token broker suits only unauthenticated or bounded public-information agents; the Google-hosted public broker is never used where tools can reach member data. End-user authentication does not make tool execution user-scoped, which remains the service agent's, so per-member data scoping must still be enforced in the backend against a session-bound identifier. Configure authorized JavaScript origins and redirect URIs to the organization's domains only. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions/ces |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(d) (Person or Entity Authentication), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: IA-2 (Identification and Authentication (Organizational Users)), IA-8 (Identification and Authentication (Non-organizational Users)), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.q (User Identification and Authentication), 01.j (User Authentication for External Connections), 01.c (Privilege Management); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API2 (Broken Authentication) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000540.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A self-hosted token broker **SHOULD** be used in preference to the Google-hosted broker for any agent application that serves unauthenticated callers or bounded public information. |
| Rationale | A self-hosted broker runs in the organization's own project on Cloud Run, which puts token issuance under the organization's logging, rate limiting, IP controls, and change management. The Google-hosted broker exists to enable public access and offers no organization-defined logic before a token is minted beyond its optional origin and reCAPTCHA checks. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Google publishes sample code for the self-hosted broker, and its service account is granted the Customer Engagement Suite Client role. That service account should hold only that role and nothing else, and it needs no downloaded key because Cloud Run attaches the identity. Because the Client role also includes tools.execute, keep issued tokens as short-lived as the broker allows. Broker request logs are useful evidence of who initiated a conversation and when. A service-account broker is not end-user authentication: every session it issues carries the same service identity, so agents serving authenticated members authenticate each member through the organization's identity provider instead. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000550.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The web widget **MUST** be embedded only on web origins the organization controls. |
| Rationale | The widget is deployed as an HTML snippet, which is copyable and will function wherever it is pasted unless an origin restriction constrains it. An agent embedded on a third-party or attacker-controlled page presents the organization's brand and voice in a context the organization cannot vouch for, and any data the member provides transits a page the organization does not control. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enforce the origin restriction with the control that matches the widget's authentication mode: the Authorized JavaScript origins of the OAuth2 client, origin validation in a self-hosted token broker or custom authentication API, or the optional origin check of the Google-hosted broker where public access is approved. Maintain the approved origin list as configuration under change control, and review it whenever a partner or vendor site is added, since an origin approved for a marketing campaign rarely gets removed afterwards. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000560.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Non-production agent development and testing **MUST** be performed in a separate Google Cloud project from production, using deployment environments that carry no production traffic. |
| Rationale | Testing an agent means driving it with realistic input against realistic tools, and in a shared project that means the test agent inherits the production service agent's reach into production backends. Deployment environments separate versions within a project but do not separate the identity that tools execute as, so the environment feature alone does not create the boundary. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Non-production projects should carry synthetic conversation data rather than copies of member conversations. This is also where the pre-release v1beta and v1alpha1 API surfaces belong, since evaluation capabilities that are only offered there can be exercised without placing production traffic on an unsupported API version. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000570.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Traffic splitting **MUST NOT** direct production conversations to an agent version that has not completed security review and evaluation. |
| Rationale | Traffic splitting was announced in the CX Agent Studio release notes on July 1, 2026 and divides user conversations in a deployment channel across several agent application versions by percentage. Google presents it both for A/B testing and to "safely deploy updates in stages", so it acts as a release control as well as an experimentation control, and it will place real member conversations on whatever version a split names, reviewed or not. The split is configured on the deployment resource rather than in the agent definition, so reviewing the agent alone does not reveal it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Require that every version participating in a split has passed review and evaluation, so the split decides how much traffic a version receives rather than whether an unreviewed version receives any. Split changes are UpdateDeployment calls, which Google logs as Data Access (DATA_WRITE) audit events rather than Admin Activity, so Data Access audit logging must be enabled for ces.googleapis.com before split changes can be alerted on. The traffic-splitting documentation configures splits through the v1beta API and the release note does not state a launch stage; confirm the stage before relying on the capability for production releases. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/traffic-split; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000580.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Third-party platforms that carry member conversations to or from a deployment channel - contact center providers such as AudioCodes, Five9, SecureCo, and Twilio, messaging platforms such as WhatsApp and Instagram, and any comparable intermediary - **MUST** have completed a third-party security assessment and, where they process ePHI, be covered by an executed business associate agreement. |
| Rationale | These platforms carry the full audio and text of member conversations between the member and the agent, which places them squarely in the ePHI path as business associates. Google's BAA covers Google Cloud services; it does not extend to a telephony, CCaaS, or messaging provider the organization contracts with separately, so the contractual chain has a gap unless the organization closes it directly. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Assess the recording, retention, and subprocessor posture of each provider, not only its transport encryption, because these platforms frequently retain their own copy of call audio on their own schedule. Confirm which party holds the recording of record and where it is stored, since that copy is outside every Google Cloud control applied to the agent. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa; https://cloud.google.com/terms/hipaa-baa; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/secureco; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/whatsapp |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000590.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Direct API access to deployed agent applications **MUST** be routed through the organization's API gateway rather than exposed to callers as a direct Google endpoint. |
| Rationale | The API channel is a conversational inference endpoint reachable by anything holding a token permitted to call runSession. The product's native controls at that boundary are project-scoped rather than per-caller: quotas such as RunSession LLM tokens per minute apply per project, base model, and region and are shared across all applications in the project; RunSession calls are recorded as Data Access audit events once those logs are enabled; and guardrails screen model input and output for content rather than authorizing callers. None of these distinguishes one API client from another, so fronting the channel with the gateway is what provides per-caller throttling, per-caller authorization policy, and request-level logging tied to the calling integration. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The gateway is also the enforcement point for any per-caller authorization the backend cannot express, and its access logs are the only record of API-channel conversations that exists independently of Google's own logging. Apply the same rate limits to internal callers as to external ones - a runaway internal integration produces the same cost and the same load. Inbound A2A invocation, where the A2A Protocol is enabled, is a direct call to the same Google endpoint by a principal holding the Client role and falls under this requirement. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/api-access; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/quotas; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/guardrail; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/a2a-protocol |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-5 (Denial-of-service Protection), AU-2 (Event Logging); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM10 (Unbounded Consumption); OWASP API Top 10 2023: API4 (Unrestricted Resource Consumption), API2 (Broken Authentication) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000600.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Voice channels **MUST** present a recording and automated-interaction disclosure to the member before conversation content is captured. |
| Rationale | Where audio recording, conversation logging, or CX Insights ingestion is enabled on a voice channel, call audio or its transcript is captured and retained: CX Agent Studio routes raw audio to a customer-owned Cloud Storage bucket when audio recording is configured, and CX Insights ingests call audio and transcripts for analysis. Recording consent is governed by state law, with two-party consent jurisdictions imposing the stricter standard, so the disclosure has to be delivered before capture begins rather than alongside it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The disclosure must cover both recording and the automated nature of the agent, and it must be spoken on voice channels rather than assumed from a web page. Where a third-party platform plays the disclosure, confirm that it does so before audio reaches Google Cloud, not after. The legal basis is state wiretap and call-recording law rather than the HIPAA Privacy Rule; confirm the applicable jurisdictions and the disclosure wording with counsel. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history |
| Mappings | NIST 800-53: PT-4 (Consent), PT-5 (Privacy Notice), PT-2 (Authority to Process Personally Identifiable Information); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001300.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Quota caps and cost alerting **MUST** be configured for every agent application exposed on a public or high-volume deployment channel. |
| Rationale | An agent application is a metered generative inference endpoint, and the web widget can be configured for anonymous public access. An attacker, a misconfigured integration, or a runaway retry loop consumes model capacity and incurs cost at a rate bounded only by the platform's own quotas, and the failure is financial and availability-affecting before it is ever a confidentiality problem. The organization's API gateway constrains the API channel but sits nowhere in the path of the web widget or the telephony connections. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  CX Agent Studio quotas apply per project, region, and base model and are shared across all applications in the project, so a ceiling for one channel requires placing high-exposure channels - the public widget in particular - in their own project. Alert on rate of spend rather than absolute spend so a change in pattern surfaces within hours rather than at the end of the billing period. Origin checks and reCAPTCHA reduce the frequency of automated abuse on the widget but place no ceiling on it. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy/web-widget; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/quotas |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001400.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | WhatsApp and Instagram deployments **MUST NOT** be created for any Gemini Enterprise for CX agent application. |
| Rationale | Google documents that only owners of the associated project can create a WhatsApp or Instagram deployment, so the channel cannot be enabled without the basic Owner role, which carries every permission in the project and which this guide prohibits on projects hosting the suite. The channel also routes member conversations through Meta, a messaging platform outside Google Cloud and outside Google's BAA, and the deployment page describes connecting Meta business assets without saying how Meta processes or retains the conversation content. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Lift this prohibition only when Google documents a creation path that does not need the Owner role, Meta has completed a third-party security assessment and, where member PHI would cross the channel, is covered by an executed business associate agreement, and the channel has been added to the deployment channel inventory with an approving owner. Check existing projects for WhatsApp and Instagram deployments at each review, because the option appeared in the console on 2026-09-24. |

## Agent Assist & Human Agent Support

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000610.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Customer-managed encryption keys **MUST** be initialized for Agent Assist before it processes any production conversation. |
| Rationale | Agent Assist maintains its own CMEK initialization separate from CX Agent Studio and CX Insights, so a project can be fully CMEK-covered on two components and unprotected on the third. Agent Assist holds live conversation transcripts, generated summaries, and knowledge excerpts, which is the same class of content the other two components protect. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Immutable at first use: Google states "You cannot change encryption key settings for a location once it has been specified" and that a project already holding Agent Assist data in a location cannot have a key added for that location. Initialize through the InitializeEncryptionSpec API and grant Cloud KMS CryptoKey Encrypter/Decrypter to service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ccai-cmek.iam.gserviceaccount.com. Coverage is not total: Google states "CMEK is not available for features that are disabled in Agent Assist locations and smart reply", so Smart Reply data is outside the customer-key boundary even in a fully CMEK-configured project. Record that exclusion in the data flow record and weigh it when deciding whether Smart Reply is enabled at all. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-28(1) (Cryptographic Protection), SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000620.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Sensitive Data Protection redaction **MUST** be configured for Agent Assist conversation data using an inspect template and a deidentify template. |
| Rationale | Agent Assist operates on live conversation content in which members state identifiers, conditions, and payment details conversationally rather than in structured fields. Redaction configured through DLP templates is the mechanism that scrubs those values before persistence, and without it the raw utterance is what is stored, analyzed, and later exported. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Redaction fails open and is bound per conversation profile, not per project: Google states "You must specify the security settings to apply for each conversation profile" and that without them "Agent Assist doesn't apply any redaction for that conversation profile." Security settings are configured per project and location but take effect only where attached, so verify by enumerating every conversation profile and confirming an attachment on each; a project can hold both templates and still store unredacted transcripts. The inspect template defines what counts as sensitive data and the deidentify template how it is masked or removed; tune both against real transcripts, since contact center language differs from the text DLP detectors are calibrated on. Google documents that Sensitive Data Protection templates outside the VPC Service Controls perimeter cause redaction failures for CX Agent Studio; Agent Assist behaviour is undocumented, so keep its templates in the same perimeter. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/data-redaction-retention; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration), SI-19 (De-identification), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000630.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent Assist conversation data retention **MUST** be configured to the shortest period that meets a documented business need. |
| Rationale | Google documents that Agent Assist security settings carry a retention_window_days value and that "The default and maximum retention window is 30 days." Because the product caps retention at 30 days, the exposure is bounded in time, unlike stores that keep conversations indefinitely unless an expiry is set; the residual risk is that the default sits at the ceiling, so unless a shorter value is chosen deliberately every conversation's transcripts and suggestions are kept for the longest period the product allows. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set retention deliberately against a stated business and records-retention rationale, using retention_window_days in the project-and-location security settings. Thirty days is a ceiling rather than a starting point, so confirm that quality review, dispute resolution, and any downstream export can operate inside the chosen window. A business need longer than 30 days cannot be met by changing this setting and must be met by exporting to a store the organization controls, which then carries its own retention obligation. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/data-redaction-retention |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), MP-6 (Media Sanitization); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000640.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Generated conversation summaries **MUST** be reviewed by the human representative before they are committed to a system of record. |
| Rationale | A summarization model produces a fluent account of the conversation whether or not it captured the material facts, and it may omit a commitment made to the member or attribute a statement to the wrong party. Once a summary is written into a case, a claim note, or a service record, it becomes the organization's account of the interaction and is relied on by every downstream reader. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The reviewing representative needs the transcript alongside the summary and a genuine ability to edit it, not merely to accept it. Writing a summary to a system of record without representative review is outside this requirement; if a business case for it arises, treat it as a documented exception approved by the record owner, with the summaries labeled as machine-generated in the record. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/summarization-with-custom-sections; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(c)(1) (Integrity); NIST 800-53: SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM09 (Misinformation) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000650.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Smart Reply, AI coach, and other generated response suggestions **MUST NOT** be transmitted to a member without an affirmative action by the human representative. |
| Rationale | Smart Reply surfaces suggested responses calculated by a custom model trained on the organization's own conversation data, and AI coach uses a large language model to suggest how the representative should respond. A suggestion that is accurate in most cases will be wrong in some. Auto-sending removes the only check in the path, and the resulting message reaches the member carrying the organization's authority with no one having read it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Affirmative means a deliberate send action, not a timeout that sends by default and not a single click that both accepts and transmits without displaying the text. Confirm the behavior in the deployed UI module rather than in the product description, because the integration layer determines what the representative actually sees. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/smart-reply; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/ai-coach-overview; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI), 164.312(c)(1) (Integrity); NIST 800-53: SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SI-10 (Information Input Validation); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information); OWASP LLM Top 10 2025: LLM06 (Excessive Agency); OWASP Agentic Top 10 2026: ASI09 (Human-Agent Trust Exploitation) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000660.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Knowledge bases used by Knowledge Assist and Generative Knowledge Assist **MUST** be scoped to content the entire representative population is authorized to see. |
| Rationale | Knowledge Assist surfaces excerpts from the configured knowledge base directly in the representative's workspace during a live call. Google documents metadata document filters (a SearchConfig in the knowledge search request) that narrow what generative knowledge assist retrieves, but the filter is part of the request the integration sends, and Google does not describe it as bound to the representative's identity, so it is not an authorization boundary. Any document in the corpus is therefore potentially visible to every representative the moment a conversation makes it relevant, regardless of the access controls that governed it in its original location. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Review the corpus against the representative population rather than against the author's intent. Material scoped to clinical staff, legal, or a specific business unit does not belong in a general contact center knowledge base, and its presence there is a disclosure that no access review of the source system will detect. Content restricted to a narrower audience must be excluded from the corpus entirely; metadata filters can reduce irrelevant results but do not enforce who may see a document. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/generative-knowledge-assist; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/ka-filters; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-21 (Information Sharing), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000670.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Live translation output **MUST NOT** be relied upon as the authoritative record of a member interaction. |
| Rationale | Agent Assist live audio-to-audio translation, a limited-access private GA feature, translates speech between the member and the representative in real time and synthesizes the translated response for the other party. Machine translation of a conversation about benefits, coverage, or clinical detail can invert meaning in ways neither party detects, because neither participant hears both languages. If only the translated side is retained, the organization holds no record of what the member actually said and cannot reconstruct the interaction in a dispute or a grievance. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Retain the source-language audio or transcript alongside the translation and mark translated turns, so the interaction can be reconstructed in a dispute. Where the interaction determines a benefit or a clinical pathway, a qualified human interpreter remains the appropriate mechanism, and language access obligations are not satisfied by a machine translation feature. The overview page does not describe what is retained from a translated conversation; confirm with Google before enablement. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/live-a2a-translation-overview; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000680.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Agent Assist deployments **MUST** be pinned to a region consistent with the organization's data residency position, with per-feature residency coverage verified before enablement. |
| Rationale | Agent Assist maintains its own regionalization documentation separate from CX Agent Studio's two multi-regions and CX Insights' region list, which means the three components can be pinned inconsistently and produce a data path that crosses a boundary the organization believes it has closed. Agent Assist also documents several features whose processing or storage does not follow the regional pin. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Configure regionalization through the API only, since "The Agent Assist console doesn't support regionalization." Record the region, the per-feature availability table, and these documented exceptions as certification evidence before enablement. (1) Vertex AI does not support the us multi-region, so generative features pinned to us use the existing US single-region endpoints. (2) The AI/ML data location commitment applies only in US and EU locations and not to non-AI/ML data in use or in transit, so residency is an at-rest guarantee. (3) Model training does not support regionalization and may route data outside the region, affecting features trained on the organization's conversations such as Smart Reply. (4) Data stores for generative knowledge assist are supported only in global, us, and eu. (5) Grounding with Google Search does not support data residency. (6) Preview Gemini Transcribe Live remaps traffic to the Vertex AI global region, moving live audio outside any regional pin. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/regionalization; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/region; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/regionalization; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/gemini-transcribe-live |
