| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000690.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Where Agent Assist data about identified human representatives - for example, AI coach suggestion usage or conversations passed to CX Insights for Quality AI scoring - is used to evaluate representative performance, that evaluation **MUST** be governed by the organization's employee monitoring and workforce policy before enablement. |
| Rationale | AI coach suggests how a representative should respond; it does not itself score the representative. Evaluation arises when Agent Assist conversations and suggestion interactions are attributed to named representatives and fed into performance processes, most directly through Quality AI, which Google describes as automating conversation scoring "to more accurately evaluate agent performance", and its agent engagement platform, which summarizes an individual agent's performance. That is continuous algorithmic evaluation of identified employees, which carries notice obligations, consultation expectations in some jurisdictions, and a fairness and contestability obligation that sits outside the security review. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Confirm before enablement who can see an individual's scores, how long they are retained, whether they feed employment decisions, and what the appeal path is. This control covers the Agent Assist side of the data flow; the Quality AI scorecards and assessments built in CX Insights need the same workforce-policy approval, and one approval can satisfy both when it names both data sources. The agent engagement platform is Preview. The technical controls protecting the conversation data say nothing about the appropriateness of the evaluation built on top of it, which is a separate approval. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/ai-coach-overview; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/qai-overview; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/agent-engagement; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist |

## Conversation Analytics & Insights

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000700.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The CX Insights encryption specification **MUST** be initialized with a customer-managed key for every location before any conversation is ingested into that location. |
| Rationale | Google states plainly that you cannot change encryption key settings for a location after that location has been enabled for CMEK, and that a project already containing CX Insights data cannot be configured for customer-managed keys retroactively. This is the single most consequential irreversible decision in the suite: getting it wrong means every conversation in that location is encrypted under Google-managed keys permanently, and the only remedy is a new project. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Initialize through the InitializeEncryptionSpec API before the first ingestion, including before any proof-of-concept upload. Create the Insights service identity first (gcloud beta services identity create --service=contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com), then grant Cloud KMS CryptoKey Encrypter/Decrypter to `service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ccai-cmek.iam.gserviceaccount.com`. CX Agent Studio manages its keys through the Conversational Insights API, and conversation history logged by CX Agent Studio is stored for CX Insights, so the first logged test conversation from an agent application may count as CX Insights data for that location; initialize the specification before any agent testing with conversation logging enabled. CMEK is not available for the `global` location, which is a further reason to pin a specific region rather than accepting the default endpoint. Google warns that if the key is revoked for more than 30 days, the Insights data encrypted by that key will be lost. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/regionalization; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-28(1) (Cryptographic Protection), SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000710.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A project-level `conversation_ttl` **MUST** be configured in CX Insights before the first conversation is ingested. |
| Rationale | Google documents that a conversation not set to expire remains in CX Insights indefinitely, and that project-level TTL only applies to conversations created after the TTL is set - changing it does not retroactively change the expiration of existing conversations. Conversations ingested during a pilot before anyone set a TTL therefore persist until someone locates and deletes them explicitly. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set the TTL as part of project initialization alongside the encryption specification, not as a later hardening step. Where both `expire_time` and `ttl` are set on a conversation, `expire_time` wins. Deletion occurs 24 hours after the expiration time and is not recoverable, so validate the value against the records-retention schedule before ingestion begins rather than after. Conversations that predate the TTL can be removed with the bulk delete method, which selects conversations by filter. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/ttl; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.conversations/bulkDelete |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), MP-6 (Media Sanitization), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000720.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Sensitive Data Protection redaction **MUST** be applied on every CX Insights ingestion path before production conversations are imported - through RedactionConfig for upload and ingest paths, and in the upstream Agent Assist or CX Agent Studio configuration for runtime integrations. |
| Rationale | CX Insights persists complete conversation transcripts and runs machine learning analysis over them, producing sentiment scores, entities, topics, and assessments that are themselves derived from the raw content. Redaction at ingestion is the control that prevents identifiers entering that corpus in the first place, and every derived artifact inherits whatever was or was not redacted at that moment. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Configure RedactionConfig with a DLP inspect template and deidentify template. Google documents that these settings "are applied to conversations ingested from the UploadConversation and IngestConversations endpoints, including conversation coming from CCAI Platform" and "are not applied to conversations ingested from the CreateConversation endpoint or the Dialogflow / Agent Assist runtime integrations", where redaction is performed in Dialogflow or Agent Assist instead. Inventory every ingestion path, confirm the redaction mechanism for each, and verify with a test conversation containing synthetic identifiers that they are absent from the stored transcript before enabling production ingestion. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/reference/rest/v1/RedactionConfig; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/data-redaction-retention |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration), SI-19 (De-identification), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000730.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Project-level CX Insights access - `roles/contactcenterinsights.viewer`, `roles/contactcenterinsights.editor`, and `roles/contactcenterinsights.admin` granted directly, and the same access inherited through any CX Agent Studio role (`roles/ces.*`) - **MUST** be held only by a named, minimal, documented group. |
| Rationale | Fine-grained access control through authorized views is Pre-GA, so there is no supported way to restrict an analyst to a subset of conversations. Every holder of project-level CX Insights access therefore sees every conversation in the project, which for a contact center deployment is the complete corpus of member interactions. Google's CX Agent Studio access-control page shows that the Viewer role (`roles/ces.viewer`) has the CX Insights Viewer role (`roles/contactcenterinsights.viewer`) as a base role and is itself the base of every CX Agent Studio editor role, and that the Admin role (`roles/ces.admin`) includes the CX Insights Admin role, so every agent builder holds conversation read access unless builder access is itself restricted. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enumerate effective access rather than direct grants: include every principal holding a `roles/ces.*` role in the project as well as direct CX Insights grants. Where agent builders do not need conversation access, develop agents in a project that holds no production conversations. Because the finer boundary does not exist in a supported form, the compensating controls are the size of the group and the visibility of its activity: keep the group small and named, review it quarterly, and rely on Data Access audit logging to record who read what. State the residual risk explicitly in the certification rather than implying a per-conversation boundary that the product does not provide. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions/ces |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.308(a)(4) (Information Access Management), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege), AC-2 (Account Management), AU-2 (Event Logging); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.c (Privilege Management), 01.v (Information Access Restriction) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000740.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | CX Insights authorized views **MUST NOT** be applied to conversation data containing PHI while the capability is Pre-GA, or configured with an empty filter expression in any project. |
| Rationale | Authorized views are Pre-GA, and Google's HIPAA guidance says "Do not use Pre-GA offerings ... in connection with PHI, unless expressly noted otherwise in a notice or other terms of the offering." Google also warns directly that empty filters or an empty string allow unrestricted access. An authorized view with an empty filter looks like a scoping control in the IAM policy and in any access review, while granting exactly the access it appears to constrain - a failure mode that is invisible precisely because the mechanism appears to be present. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Evaluate authorized views in a project holding synthetic or non-PHI conversations until the capability is generally available. Google also warns that granting authorized view set permissions at the project level results in users having access to all authorized view sets, which defeats the mechanism a second way. Grant on the specific view or view set, never at project level, and use the `agent_id` field for representative and team scoping. Authorized views cannot import or edit conversation data, train topic models, or create scorecards, so they do not replace the project-level roles for those tasks. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), AC-6 (Least Privilege), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 01.v (Information Access Restriction) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000750.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The CX Insights global endpoint **MUST NOT** be used for production conversation data. |
| Rationale | Google documents that CX Insights uses the legacy location ID `us-central1` for the global endpoint and that CMEK is not available for the `global` location. Using it therefore forfeits customer-managed encryption entirely while presenting no visible indication that it has done so, because the hostname carries no region and the default client configuration reaches it. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Both the endpoint host and the resource location path must be changed together - Google states you need to change both the host of the URL and the resource location to use the regional instance. Google does not document what happens when only one is changed; treat a mismatch as a misconfiguration. In Python clients this means setting the ClientOptions api_endpoint and updating the parent parameter; verify both in code review rather than in configuration documentation. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/regionalization; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SA-9(5) (Processing, Storage, and Service Location), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), SA-9 (External System Services); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000760.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud Storage buckets holding conversation audio for CX Insights **MUST** be encrypted with customer-managed keys, located in the approved region, and placed inside the service perimeter. |
| Rationale | Call audio is the richest single artifact in this suite: it carries the member's voice, everything they said before any redaction was applied, and background context that no transcript captures. It lands in a customer-owned Cloud Storage bucket whose security posture is entirely the organization's responsibility and is governed by none of the CX Insights settings. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where VPC Service Controls protects CX Agent Studio, Google documents that audio recordings cannot be written to Cloud Storage buckets outside the perimeter, so perimeter membership is a functional requirement for that path as well as a hardening one. Set a Cloud KMS key as the bucket default key so objects written by the service are encrypted with it; Google documents that "By default, objects in a bucket use the bucket default key." Apply a lifecycle policy consistent with the conversation TTL, since the bucket has no relationship to the CX Insights expiry and will otherwise retain audio after the analyzed conversation is gone. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://cloud.google.com/storage/docs/encryption/customer-managed-keys |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-28(1) (Cryptographic Protection), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000770.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud Speech-to-Text **MUST** be configured with customer-managed encryption keys where it transcribes conversation audio for CX Insights or Agent Assist. |
| Rationale | Google states directly that features involving external data transfer need separate CMEK configuration in the corresponding services, naming Cloud Speech-to-Text for audio transcription. Speech-to-Text is a distinct service with its own encryption configuration, so a fully CMEK-covered CX Insights deployment still passes every second of member audio through a transcription service under Google-managed keys unless it is configured separately. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  This is one of the most easily missed gaps in the suite because the transcription step is invisible in the CX Insights console - it appears as a transcript that simply exists. Include Speech-to-Text in the CMEK inventory and in the perimeter, and verify coverage by inspecting the service configuration rather than inferring it from the CX Insights encryption specification. Agent Assist conversation profiles can select the Gemini Transcribe Live model, which Google describes as using "the Gemini model for Speech-to-Text API"; record in the CMEK inventory which transcription model each conversation profile uses. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/gemini-transcribe-live |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-28(1) (Cryptographic Protection), SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000780.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | BigQuery datasets receiving the CX Insights conversation export **MUST** be encrypted with customer-managed keys, located in the approved region, and placed inside the service perimeter. |
| Rationale | Google states that BigQuery needs its own CMEK configuration for conversation exports. The export is a full copy of analyzed conversation content in a general-purpose analytics store governed by BigQuery IAM rather than by CX Insights roles, which means the carefully limited CX Insights access group has no bearing on who can query the exported copy. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Reconcile the BigQuery dataset's IAM against the approved CX Insights access group - a broader BigQuery grant silently widens access to the same conversation content. Google does not document whether deleting a conversation in CX Insights removes its exported row; assume it does not, and apply a table expiration or partition expiration consistent with the conversation TTL. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/export |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control), 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 01.v (Information Access Restriction) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000790.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | CX Insights Pub/Sub notifications for triggers whose message data is a Conversation resource - create-conversation and update-conversation, including when all events are selected - **MUST NOT** be routed to a topic whose subscribers are not approved to receive member conversation content. |
| Rationale | Google documents that for the create-conversation and update-conversation triggers the Pub/Sub message data is the Conversation itself, while the other triggers carry an operation name. The notification payload is not configurable, so the only controls are which triggers are enabled and who can subscribe, and subscribers are governed by Pub/Sub IAM rather than by any CX Insights control. A topic carrying conversation resources becomes an uncontrolled distribution channel for member conversation content into systems that were never assessed. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Prefer triggers that carry only an operation name, and have subscribers call back through the CX Insights API, where CX Insights IAM applies, to obtain content. Where a conversation trigger is required, restrict topic and subscription IAM to approved service identities and keep the topic in the approved region and perimeter. Review topic and subscription IAM alongside the CX Insights access review, since a subscription created for an integration project typically outlives it. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/pub-sub |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000800.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The CX Insights Google Sheets integration **MUST NOT** be used in any project that holds member conversation data. |
| Rationale | The Sheets integration is Pre-GA, and Google's HIPAA guidance says "Do not use Pre-GA offerings ... in connection with PHI, unless expressly noted otherwise in a notice or other terms of the offering." It moves CX Insights data into a spreadsheet shared with the CX Insights service account and governed by Google Workspace sharing rather than by Google Cloud IAM, and Google's Cloud HIPAA guidance states that HIPAA compliance for Google Workspace is covered separately. A single link change converts a controlled dataset into a broadly shared one, and the copy is beyond the reach of any retention setting, TTL, or deletion request in CX Insights. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Where analysts need tabular access, direct them to the CMEK-protected BigQuery export inside the perimeter, which retains IAM control and expiration. Aggregate reporting that must reach a spreadsheet is produced from BigQuery through a separate, reviewed process rather than through this integration. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/google-sheets-integration; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000810.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Topic models, scorecards, summarization generators, and other artifacts trained or derived from conversation content **MUST** be treated as carrying the sensitivity of the source conversations. |
| Rationale | CX Insights generates a layer of derived artifacts from the conversation corpus - inferred topics, assessment results, trained topic models. These are routinely treated as aggregate analytics and shared more freely than transcripts, but they are computed from member conversation and can retain identifying detail, particularly for rare topics where a category may correspond to a single member. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Apply the same access group, retention, and residency treatment to derived artifacts as to the conversations. Pay particular attention to sparse categories: a topic matching a small number of conversations is effectively a pointer to those members. Aggregation alone does not make an artifact de-identified under HIPAA 164.514(a); treat derived artifacts as protected health information unless that standard has actually been met. Note that authorized views cannot be used to train topic models or create scorecards, so these artifacts are produced with project-level access to the full corpus. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/release-notes |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary), 164.514(a) (De-identification of PHI); NIST 800-53: AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), AC-21 (Information Sharing), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000820.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Where Quality AI assessments evaluate identified human representatives, their use **MUST** be approved under the organization's workforce monitoring policy before enablement. |
| Rationale | Quality AI produces scorecards and assessments of representative performance from conversation content, which is algorithmic evaluation of identified employees at a scale manual review never reached. That carries notice, retention, and contestability obligations distinct from the security controls protecting the conversation data itself, and assessment results are personnel data with their own access constraints. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Determine before enablement who can see individual scores, how long assessments are retained, whether they inform employment decisions, and how a representative contests one. This control covers Quality AI scorecards, assessments, and the agent engagement platform in CX Insights; the Agent Assist data that feeds them needs the same workforce-policy approval, and one approval can satisfy both when it names both data sources. The agent engagement platform is Preview, and multiple scorecards remains a Preview capability, so any design depending on either rests on a Pre-GA feature. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000830.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Conversations ingested into CX Insights during pilot or proof-of-concept activity **MUST** be deleted before the production deployment is certified, unless they were ingested under the full production control set. |
| Rationale | Pilot conversations are ingested before the controls are finished, which is the point of a pilot. If the encryption specification was not initialized, they are permanently under Google-managed keys; if no TTL was set they persist indefinitely, because the project-level TTL is not retroactive; and if redaction was not configured they hold unredacted member content that every subsequent analysis inherits. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enumerate and delete pilot conversations explicitly - for example with the bulk delete method and a filter that selects the pilot period - rather than assuming a later TTL will collect them; Google is explicit that changing the TTL does not retroactively change the expiration of existing conversations. Where the encryption specification was not set correctly at pilot time, deleting the conversations does not fix the location, and a new project is the remedy. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/ttl; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/reference/rest/v1/projects.locations.conversations/bulkDelete |

## Data Protection & Privacy

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000840.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | CX Agent Studio **MUST** be CMEK-integrated at project creation, before any agent application or conversation data exists in the project. |
| Rationale | Google states that existing resources in non-CMEK integrated projects cannot be CMEK integrated retroactively, and recommends that resources be exported and restored in a new project for CMEK. A project that begins without CMEK therefore holds agent instructions, tool configurations, application variables, and conversation history under Google-managed keys until the deployment is exported and restored into a new CMEK-integrated project, which is a migration rather than a setting change. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Treat CMEK integration as a project provisioning step in the same class as enabling the API, not as a hardening task. Google notes that one key should be used per project location, and that CX Agent Studio key management (create, enable, and revoke) is handled by using the Conversational Insights API. For CX Insights, create the Insights service identity, grant service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ccai-cmek.iam.gserviceaccount.com the Cloud KMS CryptoKey Encrypter/Decrypter role, and call InitializeEncryptionSpec for the location; Google warns that a customer-managed key cannot be configured for a project that already has CX Insights data. Because conversation history is surfaced through CX Insights, assume that a logged test conversation may create Insights data, and complete this step first. Verify with GetEncryptionSpec before the first agent application is created. Google also warns that if a key is revoked for more than 30 days, the Insights data encrypted by that key will be lost. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption), 164.312(e)(2)(ii) (Encryption); NIST 800-53: SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SC-28(1) (Cryptographic Protection), SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information), 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000850.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud KMS keys protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX data **MUST** be monitored for availability, with key destruction and disablement gated on multi-party authorization. |
| Rationale | Google warns that if a key is revoked for more than 30 days, the CX Insights data encrypted by that key will be lost. This converts an ordinary key management error - a mistaken disable, an over-eager cleanup of an apparently unused key, an expired rotation schedule - into permanent destruction of the member conversation corpus on a 30-day fuse, with no recovery path once it burns. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Alert on key state changes and on decryption failures against these keys, and treat any such alert as an availability incident with a hard 30-day clock rather than a configuration ticket. Protect key destruction with organization policy and a separate approval, and confirm the KMS key's own IAM excludes the people who administer the CX projects. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption), 164.308(a)(7) (Contingency Plan); NIST 800-53: SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), SC-12(1) (Availability), CM-5 (Access Restrictions for Change), AC-5 (Separation of Duties), SI-4 (System Monitoring); HITRUST CSF v11: 10.g (Key Management), 09.ab (Monitoring System Use) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000860.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud KMS key versions protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX data **MUST NOT** be disabled or destroyed while any data encrypted under them is still retained. |
| Rationale | Google states for CX Agent Studio that key rotation is supported but data re-encryption is not: re-encrypting previously encrypted data with a new key version is not supported. Rotation therefore protects only newly written data, and previously written conversation content stays bound to the older key version for as long as it is retained. A rotation standard that retires old key versions on a schedule makes that data unreadable, which is the same permanent loss that follows revoking the key itself. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Retain every key version for as long as any data encrypted under it is retained, which means key version lifetime is governed by the conversation retention schedule rather than by the rotation period. Document this explicitly, because it contradicts the default assumption behind most rotation standards. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(a)(2)(iv) (Encryption and Decryption); NIST 800-53: SC-12 (Cryptographic Key Establishment and Management), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention); HITRUST CSF v11: 10.g (Key Management) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000870.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | CX Agent Studio agent data **MUST** be located in the `us` multi-region. |
| Rationale | CX Agent Studio offers exactly two multi-regions, `us` and `eu`, and the region governs where callback code, tool code and configurations, agent instructions and prompts, application variables, and conversation history are stored at rest. Member conversation content for a US population belongs in the US multi-region both to satisfy the organization's residency position and to keep the data path within a single legal jurisdiction. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  A region must be supplied on every API request through the location path or resource parameter, so an omitted or defaulted region in client code is a residency defect that no console setting reveals. Verify the region in deployed client configuration rather than in design documentation. Two adjacent services need separate attention: Google states that OpenAPI tools support only global Secret Manager (regional secrets are unsupported), so the storage location of tool credentials is governed by each secret's own replication policy rather than by the agent region and must be confirmed separately; and the BigQuery export dataset must exist in the same location as the agent. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/region; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(B) (Risk Management); NIST 800-53: SA-9(5) (Processing, Storage, and Service Location), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-9 (External System Services); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000880.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud Logging **MUST** be configured with a US region for log storage independently of the Gemini Enterprise for CX residency setting. |
| Rationale | Google's residency documentation for CX Agent Studio directs the reader to the Cloud Logging documentation to control where logs are stored, which means logging sits outside the product's residency guarantee. Because Cloud Logging is documented as one of the customer-owned storage paths for conversation data, service logs routed there may contain member conversation content, and an unpinned logging configuration can store that content outside the residency boundary the rest of the deployment maintains. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set the default storage location for new _Default and _Required log buckets in the Cloud Logging default resource settings at organization or folder level, so new projects inherit it, and confirm the location of existing buckets. Confirm that any log sink forwarding to a SIEM does not itself relocate the data. This is a separate configuration in a separate product, so it is easily missed in a residency review that only examines the CX components. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/region; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/regionalized-logs; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/default-settings |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(B) (Risk Management); NIST 800-53: SA-9(5) (Processing, Storage, and Service Location), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-9 (External System Services); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000890.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Conversation history retention in CX Agent Studio **MUST** be configured to a documented period no longer than the business need requires. |
| Rationale | Google documents retention as configurable with a default of one year and a maximum of two. Left at the default, the deployment accumulates a year of complete member conversation history because nobody made a decision, and the volume of that corpus is the primary determinant of the impact of any future compromise of the analytics surface. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set the value against the records-retention schedule and the operational need for dispute resolution, and record the rationale. Google states that the Log your customer conversations setting applies to both CX Agent Studio and CX Insights; separately, CX Insights documents per-conversation TTL and states that a conversation not set to expire remains in CX Insights indefinitely, so confirm with Google which mechanism governs conversations that reach CX Insights from CX Agent Studio. The Cloud Storage audio, the BigQuery export, and Cloud Logging each expire on their own schedules and must be aligned deliberately. Leaving the setting at its one-year default is not a decision. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/ttl |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), MP-6 (Media Sanitization), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000900.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The state of the Log your customer conversations setting (labeled Interaction data in agent application settings) **MUST** be an explicit, documented decision for every agent application. |
| Rationale | This single toggle governs whether long-term conversational data exists at all: Google states that disabling it disables all types of long-term conversational data except BigQuery data, and that with logging disabled conversational data does not exist in any Google-owned persistent storage to be accessed. It is simultaneously the strongest available data minimization control and a control that disables the monitoring dashboards and the CX Insights corpus that operations depends on. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Because the trade is real, make it deliberately rather than by default. Note the exception in Google's own wording - BigQuery data is not disabled by the toggle - so a deployment that turns logging off while an export remains configured has not stopped conversation content being persisted. Google's settings page labels the same control Interaction data ("Store interaction data for deeper insights and your reference"), while the conversation history page calls it Log your customer conversations. Record the decision and the exception check as certification evidence. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000910.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Written confirmation from Google that the Service Specific Terms training restriction covers every Gemini Enterprise for CX component and data path in use **MUST** be obtained and retained as certification evidence. |
| Rationale | Google's Service Specific Terms contain a contractual training restriction in section 18: "Google will not use Customer Data to train or fine-tune any AI/ML models without Customer's prior permission or instruction." The conversation history documentation separately states that customer content shared through the Improve agent performance by sharing conversation data toggle is never used to train Google's production machine learning models. Neither text names the individual components of this suite or its data paths, including audio through Speech-to-Text and analytics in CX Insights, and member conversation content used for training would be a use of PHI outside the purposes the organization has authorized. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Section 18 sits under the AI/ML Services part of the Service Specific Terms; ask Google to confirm, through the contracting relationship, that each component in use and each data path falls within it, rather than relying on a documentation sentence that Google can revise without notice. Google's HIPAA guidance also states that a customer using Cloud Speech-to-Text under a BAA should not opt into the data logging program; confirm that it is not enabled. Retain the response as evidence, and re-confirm at contract renewal and whenever a new component is enabled. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://cloud.google.com/terms/hipaa-baa; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000920.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Sensitive Data Protection templates used by any Gemini Enterprise for CX component **MUST** be located inside the same VPC Service Controls perimeter as the component that references them. |
| Rationale | Google states that Sensitive Data Protection redaction templates outside the perimeter cause failures in redacting conversation contents. Google does not document whether ingestion stops or continues when redaction fails; if it continues, unredacted member content reaches the conversation corpus and the misconfiguration surfaces later as a data problem rather than as an error at the time. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Verify template location as part of perimeter change review, and monitor for redaction failures as an operational signal rather than assuming success. Because a template can be moved or a perimeter redrawn independently of the CX configuration, re-verify after any change to either. Redacted transcripts are not de-identified under HIPAA and remain PHI. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000930.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Member identifiers and other sensitive values **MUST NOT** be written into application variables, agent instructions, or tool configuration as static values. |
| Rationale | Google's HIPAA guidance states that when creating or updating Conversational Agents agents, customers should avoid including PHI or security credentials anywhere in the agent definition. Application variables and agent instructions are documented as agent data at rest, readable by every holder of the Gemini Enterprise for CX Viewer role and carried in configuration exports. A member identifier placed there persists beyond the conversation that produced it, sits outside the conversation retention and TTL settings, and is not subject to the redaction applied to conversation content. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Pass values that vary by member at runtime through the session rather than the configuration; Google documents session context variables that can be injected into OpenAPI tool requests. Include a scan for identifier-shaped values in the pre-deployment agent review, alongside the credential scan. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000940.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A documented procedure **MUST** exist to locate and delete all copies of an individual member's conversation data across every store in the deployment. |
| Rationale | Conversation content is written to at least five documented places: the Google-managed store used by CX Agent Studio and CX Insights, CX Insights conversations, Cloud Storage audio, Cloud Logging, and the BigQuery export, plus any downstream subscriber the organization configures. Each has its own deletion mechanism and its own retention clock, and deleting a conversation in one does not remove it from the others. A deletion obligation - from the organization's records-retention schedule, from applicable state law, or from the business associate agreement's return-or-destroy clause on termination - that the organization cannot execute completely is a compliance failure regardless of intent. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Build the procedure against the store inventory rather than against the console, and test it end to end with a synthetic conversation, verifying absence in every store afterwards. CX Agent Studio provides DeleteConversation and BatchDeleteConversations operations, and CX Insights conversations can be deleted manually; the BigQuery export and Cloud Storage audio require their own steps. HIPAA does not create an individual right to deletion; record which obligation each deletion satisfies. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/ttl; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/terms/hipaa-baa |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.310(d)(2)(i) (Disposal), 164.502(b) (Minimum Necessary); NIST 800-53: SI-12 (Information Management and Retention), MP-6 (Media Sanitization), CM-8 (System Component Inventory); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.p (Disposal of Media), 06.c (Protection of Organizational Records) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000950.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The short-lived caches the product maintains **MUST** be documented in the data flow record supporting certification. |
| Rationale | Google documents a session memcache holding active session management data and metadata with a 30-minute TTL, and a Text-to-Speech Memstore cache with a one-hour TTL. These are minor exposures individually, but an incomplete data flow diagram is a finding in its own right, and an assessor who discovers an undocumented store asks what else was omitted. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Record them as transient stores with their stated TTLs rather than attempting to control them - the product exposes no configuration for either. Their presence is also the reason a deletion request cannot be confirmed complete within the first hour of a conversation ending. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000960.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Google personnel access to Gemini Enterprise for CX conversation data **SHOULD** be gated by Access Approval and recorded through Access Transparency. |
| Rationale | Google documents that support and engineering personnel may have controlled, just-in-time access to Google-managed operational data for critical debugging and system maintenance. For a corpus of member conversation content this is provider access to ePHI permitted under the business associate relationship. Google lists Access Approval approval requests as generally available for Customer Experience Agent Studio, Customer Experience Insights, and Gemini Enterprise for Customer Experience, which lets the organization decide on that access before it occurs, and lists Access Transparency as generally available for the same services, which provides the record. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Confirm with Google the entitlement required for Access Approval and Access Transparency, and route the logs to the same destination as the audit logs. Confirm per service which components of this suite are covered rather than assuming the whole deployment is, since coverage is enumerated per service and changes over time. |

## Network & Perimeter Security

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000970.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A VPC Service Controls perimeter **MUST** be configured with `ces.googleapis.com`, `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com`, and, where Agent Assist is used, `dialogflow.googleapis.com` in the restricted services list. |
| Rationale | `ces.googleapis.com` carries agent configuration, tool invocation, and live conversation; `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` carries the analyzed conversation corpus; and Agent Assist is served through `dialogflow.googleapis.com`. Any of these left unrestricted remains reachable with stolen credentials from any network, which for the Insights service means the complete member conversation corpus. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Google names `ces.googleapis.com` and `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` in its perimeter guidance for CX Agent Studio, and its VPC Service Controls supported-products list gives `dialogflow.googleapis.com` as the service name for Agent Assist. The CX Agent Studio perimeter page documents failures when Cloud Storage buckets, BigQuery datasets, Sensitive Data Protection redaction templates, Secret Manager secrets, and data stores sit outside the perimeter, so restrict `storage.googleapis.com`, `bigquery.googleapis.com`, `dlp.googleapis.com`, `secretmanager.googleapis.com`, and `discoveryengine.googleapis.com` in the same perimeter. The CX Insights entry also directs adding the Agent Platform API (`aiplatform.googleapis.com`) when integrating multiple CCAI products. Restrict Cloud KMS and Speech-to-Text where they are used as a design choice; Google does not name them as breakage points. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://cloud.google.com/vpc-service-controls/docs/supported-products |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls), 09.n (Security of Network Services) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000980.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The service perimeter **MUST** be established and enforced before agent applications, data stores, deployment channels, and CX Insights locations are created. |
| Rationale | Google documents seven distinct operations that fail once a perimeter is enforced around existing resources: tools and callbacks reaching arbitrary HTTP endpoints, Cloud Storage audio writes, BigQuery export, Sensitive Data Protection redaction templates, OpenAPI tool secrets, data store tool execution, and cross-perimeter agent references and import or export. Enforcing a perimeter after the fact therefore produces a wave of simultaneous production failures at exactly the moment there is pressure to write exceptions rather than relocate resources. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Google documentation does not itself state a sequencing requirement - this control is derived from the enumerated breakages rather than quoted from Google. Sequence as: define the perimeter, add restricted services, configure ingress and egress rules, validate in dry-run, enforce, then create resources. Record an owner and a removal condition for any exception written during cutover. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures), CM-4 (Impact Analyses); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-000990.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The service perimeter **MUST** be validated in dry-run mode and its findings resolved before enforcement. |
| Rationale | Dry-run mode reports what enforcement would block without blocking it, which is the only safe way to discover which of the seven documented breakage paths a given deployment actually depends on. Skipping it means discovering the same information as a production outage, and outage-driven remediation reliably produces broad exceptions instead of relocated resources. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Run the dry-run across a full operational cycle rather than a single test conversation, because several breakage paths - BigQuery export, Cloud Storage audio writes, and agent application import and export - occur only when those features are exercised. Google documents that in dry run mode requests that violate the perimeter policy are not denied, only logged, with metadata.dryRun set to True in the audit log. Resolve each finding by moving the resource inside the perimeter, not by exempting the service. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://cloud.google.com/vpc-service-controls/docs/dry-run-mode |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CA-2 (Control Assessments), CM-4 (Impact Analyses); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001000.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Tools and callbacks **MUST NOT** be able to call HTTP(S) endpoints outside the approved list, enforced through the CX Agent Studio allowed origins endpoint control policy with its enforcement scope set to ALWAYS. |
| Rationale | A tool or callback URL is an outbound channel for conversation-derived parameters. Google documents that allowed origins applies to all types of tools and callbacks which can make external requests, that when VPC Service Controls is not enabled (the default) all endpoints are allowed, and that the ALWAYS enforcement scope applies regardless of VPC-SC status. Without that policy, any holder of the Tools Editor role (for tools) or the Agent Editor role (for callbacks) can point the deployment at any host on the internet. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Set EndpointControlPolicy.enforcementScope to ALWAYS; an unspecified scope is treated as VPCSC_ONLY. Allowed origins are exact-match URLs including the scheme and optional port, with no wildcards, and an origin without a port allows all ports. The setting applies at project and location level and requires the `ces.securitySettings.update` permission, held by `roles/ces.securitySettingsEditor` and `roles/ces.admin`, which Google intends for security or project administrators separate from those who edit agent data. Reconcile the list against the tool inventory quarterly. For targets inside the organization's VPC, configure Service Directory private network access. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1beta/SecuritySettings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool/open-api; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/iam/docs/roles-permissions/ces |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001010.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Service Directory private network access **MUST** be configured for OpenAPI tool targets hosted inside the organization's VPC network. |
| Rationale | Google documents Service Directory integration as the supported path that keeps traffic within the Google Cloud network and enforces IAM and VPC Service Controls for OpenAPI tool targets. It lets a tool reach an internal system without exposing that system publicly and without breaking under perimeter enforcement; Google also lists Integration Connectors as an optional perimeter dependency for connector-based tools. |

Configure this before perimeter enforcement so tool connectivity is already on the supported path. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001020.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Cloud Storage buckets, BigQuery datasets, Secret Manager secrets, Sensitive Data Protection templates, and data stores referenced by Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** reside inside the same service perimeter as the components that reference them. |
| Rationale | Google enumerates each of these as a specific failure: audio recordings cannot be written to buckets outside the perimeter, conversation data cannot be written to external BigQuery datasets, OpenAPI tools cannot reference secrets outside the perimeter, external redaction templates cause redaction failures, and data store tools specifying a data store outside the perimeter fail to execute. A resource outside the perimeter is therefore both a security gap and a functional break. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enumerate every referenced resource before enforcement, because these references are created by different roles at different times and no single console view lists them together. The redaction template case is the most dangerous of the five, since it degrades data protection rather than producing an obvious failure. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SC-7(10) (Prevent Exfiltration); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001030.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Every ingress rule, egress rule, and allowed origin for the Gemini Enterprise for CX perimeter **MUST** name specific identities, sources, services, or URLs - never ANY_IDENTITY, an access level or service name of "*" - and carry a documented owner and a review at least annually. |
| Rationale | A VPC Service Controls perimeter denies cross-boundary requests unless an ingress or egress rule allows them, so the perimeter's effective strength is set by those rules and by the CX Agent Studio allowed origins list. Google documents that ANY_IDENTITY allows requests from all identities, including unauthenticated requests, and that an access level of "*" allows access from any network origin. Broad rules accumulate under delivery pressure, most often during the enforcement cutover, and an unreviewed rule set converts the perimeter into documentation rather than a control. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Record for each rule or origin what it permits, who requested it, and what would have to change for it to be removed. Rules added during a cutover deserve particular scrutiny because they were written to end an outage rather than to express an authorization decision. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/vpc-service-controls/docs/ingress-egress-rules; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(e)(1) (Transmission Security); NIST 800-53: SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SC-7(5) (Deny by Default - Allow by Exception), AC-4 (Information Flow Enforcement), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.m (Network Controls) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001040.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Flow-based agents **MUST NOT** reference agent resources located outside the service perimeter. |
| Rationale | Google documents that flow-based agents specifying an agent resource outside the perimeter fail when called. Beyond the functional break, a cross-perimeter agent reference delegates conversation handling across a security boundary. Google does not document the identity under which a referenced agent in another project executes; if it runs with its own project's service agent permissions, the reference extends reach into a project the calling perimeter does not govern. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Keep the full agent delegation graph inside a single perimeter and review it whenever a new agent reference is added. A reference that crosses perimeters should be treated as a design finding rather than a configuration option. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/tool |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001050.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Import and export operations for agent configuration **MUST** target Cloud Storage buckets inside the service perimeter. |
| Rationale | Google documents that import and export operations targeting external Cloud Storage buckets fail under perimeter enforcement. Export carries the complete agent configuration - instructions, tool definitions, and application variables - so an export to a bucket outside the perimeter is a full disclosure of the deployment's logic and its integration topology. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Restrict export destinations to a small set of perimeter-internal buckets under change control. Google's audit logging reference lists ExportApp under ADMIN_READ, a Data Access audit log type that is typically disabled by default, and ImportApp under ADMIN_WRITE, which is recorded in Admin Activity logs; enable the ADMIN_READ sub-type for `ces.googleapis.com` or exports will not be logged. Google also documents that requests involving Cloud Storage access use the caller's permissions, so the exporting principal needs access to the destination bucket as well. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001060.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Changes to the service perimeter protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be change controlled and alerted on. |
| Rationale | The perimeter is the compensating control for several exposures this product cannot address internally, including the service-agent tool execution model and the absence of supported per-conversation scoping in the analytics surface. Because a single policy edit can remove a service from the restricted list without any visible effect on the application, perimeter changes are both high impact and low friction. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Alert on Access Context Manager service perimeter changes - for example CreateServicePerimeter, UpdateServicePerimeter, DeleteServicePerimeter, ReplaceServicePerimeters, and CommitServicePerimeters under `protoPayload.serviceName="accesscontextmanager.googleapis.com"` - and route them to the security operations queue rather than to the platform team that made them. Reconcile the restricted service list against the documented baseline on a quarterly configuration review. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/vpc-service-controls; https://cloud.google.com/access-context-manager/docs/audit-logging |

## Monitoring & Analytics

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001070.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Data Access audit logs for `ces.googleapis.com` **MUST** be explicitly enabled for all three sub-types: ADMIN_READ, DATA_READ, and DATA_WRITE. |
| Rationale | Google documents that Admin Activity audit logs are enabled by default while Data Access logs require explicit enablement. For CX Agent Studio the Data Access tier is the one that records agent and tool operations and conversation deletion - the reads and writes touching member conversation content. Without it, the audit trail covers who created an application but not who used it or what it reached. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Enable at organization level so new projects inherit the configuration rather than depending on a project-creation checklist. Google lists ExportApp and GetSecuritySettings under ADMIN_READ, and CreateTool, UpdateTool, DeleteConversation, BatchDeleteConversations, and RunSession under DATA_WRITE, so enabling DATA_READ alone misses both exports and changes. Verify with a filter on `protoPayload.serviceName="ces.googleapis.com"` after enablement, and note that long-running operations produce two entries, one at start and one at completion. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(D) (Information System Activity Review); NIST 800-53: AU-2 (Event Logging), AU-12 (Audit Record Generation), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records), CM-6 (Configuration Settings); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.aa (Audit Logging) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001080.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Data Access audit logs for `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` **MUST** be explicitly enabled for all three sub-types: ADMIN_READ, DATA_READ, and DATA_WRITE. |
| Rationale | CX Insights holds the complete analysed conversation corpus and, because fine-grained access control is Pre-GA, every approved user can read all of it. The Data Access log is therefore the only record of which analyst read which member's conversation, which makes it the primary compensating control for the absence of a supported per-conversation access boundary. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Google lists reads such as GetConversation, ListConversations, and ExportInsightsData under DATA_READ, and nearly every mutation - including CreateAnalysis, CreateAnalysisRule, BulkDeleteConversations, and UpdateSettings - under DATA_WRITE; only SetIamPolicy is ADMIN_WRITE. Without DATA_WRITE, almost no change to CX Insights is recorded. Verify with a filter on `protoPayload.serviceName="contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com"`. Given the breadth of standing access, retention and reviewability of these logs matter more here than anywhere else in the deployment. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(D) (Information System Activity Review), 164.312(a)(1) (Access Control); NIST 800-53: AU-2 (Event Logging), AU-12 (Audit Record Generation), AU-6 (Audit Record Review, Analysis, and Reporting), AC-3 (Access Enforcement); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.aa (Audit Logging) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001090.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Audit logs from every Gemini Enterprise for CX project **MUST** be exported to the organization's security information and event management platform. |
| Rationale | Cloud Logging retention is bounded and project-scoped, and detection of misuse across a multi-project deployment requires correlation that a per-project log view cannot provide. Exporting is also what places the records outside the control of the same administrators whose actions they record. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Export Admin Activity, Data Access, and any Access Transparency logs through an aggregated sink at organization or folder level with includeChildren set, so a newly created project is covered without action. Confirm that the sink destination does not relocate the data outside the residency boundary maintained elsewhere in the deployment. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/export/aggregated_sinks; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/routing/overview |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(D) (Information System Activity Review); NIST 800-53: AU-6 (Audit Record Review, Analysis, and Reporting), AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information), SI-4 (System Monitoring), AU-4 (Audit Log Storage Capacity); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.aa (Audit Logging), 09.ab (Monitoring System Use), 09.ac (Protection of Log Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001100.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Audit log retention **MUST** meet or exceed the organization's retention requirement for records of access to protected health information. |
| Rationale | Data Access logs for these services are the evidence of who read member conversation content, and their value in an investigation or a breach determination is a direct function of how far back they reach. Google documents that Data Access audit logs are stored in the _Default bucket unless routed elsewhere, and that the _Default bucket's default retention period is 30 days. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The organization treats these audit logs as records of activity required by HIPAA 164.316(b)(1), and therefore applies the six-year period in 164.316(b)(2)(i), which requires that documentation to be retained for six years from creation or the date it was last in effect, whichever is later. The regulation does not name audit logs specifically; six years is the organization's interpretation and a conservative floor. Set retention on the log bucket (1 to 3650 days) or on the export destination rather than relying on defaults, and lock the bucket so the retention policy cannot be reduced; Google warns that locking a log bucket is irreversible. Retention on the exported copy is what matters if the project itself is compromised or deleted. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/audit/configure-data-access; https://cloud.google.com/logging/quotas; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/buckets; https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-45/subtitle-A/subchapter-C/part-164/subpart-C/section-164.316 |

| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001110.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Exported audit logs **MUST** be protected against modification and deletion by the administrators of the Gemini Enterprise for CX projects. |
| Rationale | The administrators of these projects are the population whose actions the logs record. Where the same identities can edit sinks, alter retention, or delete log buckets, the audit trail attests only to activity that the administrator chose to leave in place, which is not an audit trail. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Route the export to a project under separate administrative control with its own IAM, and restrict log bucket and sink modification to the logging or security function. Lock the destination log bucket so its retention policy cannot be reduced and the bucket cannot be deleted until its entries have met the retention period. Alert on changes to sink configuration and log retention as security events. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/routing/overview; https://cloud.google.com/logging/docs/buckets |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.312(c)(1) (Integrity); NIST 800-53: AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information), AU-9(4) (Access by Subset of Privileged Users), AC-5 (Separation of Duties), AC-6 (Least Privilege); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.ac (Protection of Log Information) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001120.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Alerts **MUST** be configured for changes to CX Agent Studio security settings and to agent application logging, redaction, and export settings. |
| Rationale | The project-level security settings hold the endpoint control policy - the allowed origins list that governs which HTTP(S) endpoints tools and callbacks can call - so a single change there widens the egress boundary of every agent application in the project and location. Redaction, Cloud Logging, BigQuery export, audio recording, and interaction data storage are agent application settings, managed by the App Editor role. Neither change produces a deployment, and neither is visible to someone reading an individual agent's configuration. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Alert on `google.cloud.ces.v1beta.AgentService.UpdateSecuritySettings` and on `AgentService.UpdateApp` (v1 and v1beta), both recorded in Admin Activity logs under `ces.googleapis.com`, and route them to the security operations queue. Correlate each alert with an approved change record and investigate any change without one. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/reference/rest/v1beta/SecuritySettings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.312(b) (Audit Controls), 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(D) (Information System Activity Review); NIST 800-53: SI-4 (System Monitoring), AU-6 (Audit Record Review, Analysis, and Reporting), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), IR-4 (Incident Handling); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.ab (Monitoring System Use), 09.ad (Administrator and Operator Logs) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001130.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Alerts **MUST** be configured for conversation deletion and bulk deletion operations in CX Agent Studio and CX Insights. |
| Rationale | Conversation deletion is both a legitimate privacy operation and the natural final step of an attacker or an insider covering their activity. CX Insights supports bulk operations over conversations, which makes large-scale destruction a single API call, and expiry-based deletion is documented as not recoverable. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Alert on DeleteConversation and the bulk delete operations in both services, and distinguish deletions performed through the documented privacy procedure from ad hoc ones. A deletion volume anomaly is worth investigating even where each individual deletion appears authorized. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/ttl |
