| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001140.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Alerts **MUST** be configured for tool registration and modification in CX Agent Studio and for changes to the tools assigned to agents. |
| Rationale | Tools execute with the CX Agent Studio service account's permissions rather than the end user's. A new tool becomes reachable once it is added to an agent, and a modified tool changes the reach of every agent already using it, so tool changes alter the deployment's effective authorization boundary without a deployment step or an approval gate in the product. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Alert on CreateTool, UpdateTool, CreateToolset, and UpdateToolset (DATA_WRITE, recorded in Data Access logs under `ces.googleapis.com`), and on UpdateAgent, through which the Agent Editor role adds tools to agents. Reconcile against the tool inventory. A registration with no corresponding inventory entry is either an undocumented integration or a compromise, and both warrant the same first question. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001150.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Alerts **MUST** be configured for anomalous read volume against CX Insights conversation data. |
| Rationale | Because every approved CX Insights user can read every conversation, the difference between legitimate analysis and bulk extraction is volume and pattern rather than authorization. No preventive control distinguishes them, so detection is the available control and it depends entirely on the Data Access log being both enabled and monitored. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Baseline normal analyst read volume before setting thresholds, and alert on deviation rather than on an absolute count, since legitimate quality review is itself high volume. Pair with the quarterly entitlement review so that an unexpected reader is noticed as well as an unexpected volume. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/audit-logging; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/best-practices-for-fine-grained-access-control |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001160.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Operational monitoring **MUST NOT** depend solely on the product's built-in conversation dashboards where conversation logging is disabled or can be disabled. |
| Rationale | Google notes that disabling the conversation logging toggle affects the monitoring dashboards, which couples the data minimization decision to the observability capability. A monitoring practice that assumes those dashboards will be populated becomes silently blind if the toggle is turned off for a privacy reason on a different team's initiative. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Base operational and security monitoring on Cloud Audit Logs and platform metrics, and treat the product dashboards as a convenience layer. Google does not state whether these sources are affected by the toggle, so confirm after any change to the logging setting that they are still populated. Record the dependency so the observability consequence is visible to whoever considers changing the logging setting. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001170.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | An incident response procedure covering Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be documented, including containment steps for a compromised agent application or tool. |
| Rationale | The containment actions in this product are specific and not obvious under pressure: restoring a previous agent application version, revoking the Gemini Enterprise for CX Client role from the principal a channel uses, removing a tool registration, deleting a deployment, locking the agent application against further changes, tightening the allowed origins list, and tightening the perimeter. A generic cloud incident procedure will not name any of them, and a live agent continues serving members throughout the delay. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Document who can execute each containment action and confirm they can reach it outside business hours. Exercise the agent version restore at least once before relying on it. Where an incident involves member conversation content, the breach determination path and the notification clock apply as they would to any other ePHI disclosure; under the BAA, Google notifies the organization of breaches it discovers (164.410), and the organization's own duties under 164.404 and 164.408 follow from discovery. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/access-control; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/settings; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/audit-logging |

## Compliance & Certification

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001180.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | A business associate agreement covering Google Cloud **MUST** be executed and its scope confirmed to include every Gemini Enterprise for CX component in use before production member conversation data is processed. |
| Rationale | Every component of this suite processes protected health information in its most unstructured and least controllable form. Google's BAA covers the infrastructure and the services named in the covered products list, and coverage attaches per product rather than per platform, so an executed BAA is a necessary but not sufficient condition and its scope must be checked against the specific components deployed. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Confirm coverage before enablement rather than at annual review, since the components are enabled independently and by different teams. Retain the confirmation as certification evidence with the date it was checked, because the covered products list is revised as products are added. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/terms/hipaa-baa; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts), 164.314(a) (Business Associate Contracts or Other Arrangements), 164.502(e) (Disclosures to Business Associates); NIST 800-53: SA-9 (External System Services), SA-4 (Acquisition Process), CA-3 (Information Exchange); HITRUST CSF v11: 05.k (Addressing Security in Third Party Agreements) |
| Detective Policies |  |
| Sentinel Modules |  |
| CSP Level Controls |   |
| Monitoring Controls |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001190.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Gemini Enterprise for CX components **MUST NOT** process protected health information unless Google's HIPAA covered products list names the component or Google confirms in writing that the component falls within a listed product. |
| Rationale | Google's covered products list is a flat list that names, verbatim, Gemini Enterprise for Customer Experience (GECX), Customer Experience Agent Studio, CX Insights, Contact Center AI Agent Assist, Contact Center AI Platform, and Conversational Agents. Because the suite name is itself an entry, the absence of a feature's own name is not conclusive either way: a generally available component may be covered through the suite entry, while a component with no GA release, such as Commerce agents, is not shown to be covered by it. Coverage therefore has to be demonstrated per component, by its own entry or by Google's written confirmation, and processing ePHI through a component that has neither is a disclosure outside the BAA. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Record, per component, the list entry or the written confirmation that establishes coverage and the date it was checked, and re-check before enablement and at each annual reassessment. As of 2026-09-24, Commerce agents does not appear in the list under its own name. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa; https://cloud.google.com/terms/hipaa-baa |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts), 164.502(e) (Disclosures to Business Associates), 164.502(a) (Uses and Disclosures of PHI); NIST 800-53: SA-9 (External System Services), CM-7 (Least Functionality), SA-4 (Acquisition Process), RA-3 (Risk Assessment); HITRUST CSF v11: 05.k (Addressing Security in Third Party Agreements) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001200.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Every Google API in the path of member conversation data, including the Dialogflow API (`dialogflow.googleapis.com`) used by Agent Assist and Conversational Agents, **MUST** be confirmed by Google in writing as within a product on Google's HIPAA covered products list before it processes protected health information. |
| Rationale | Google's HIPAA covered products list names Conversational Agents, Contact Center AI Agent Assist, Customer Experience Agent Studio, and CX Insights but does not use the name Dialogflow. The VPC Service Controls documentation gives `dialogflow.googleapis.com` as the service name for Agent Assist, CX Insights requires Dialogflow runtime integration to ingest conversations from Dialogflow and Agent Assist, and Google's SOC 2 scope lists Conversational Agents as formerly Dialogflow. Product names, API names, and list entries therefore do not line up one to one, and a deployment can route PHI through an API whose coverage has been assumed rather than confirmed. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Inventory each API in the data path by service name, map it to a covered product name, and obtain Google's written confirmation for any mapping that is not self-evident, starting with `dialogflow.googleapis.com`. Audit the CX Insights ingestion configuration for Dialogflow runtime integration and for legacy Dialogflow ES agents; Google's Dialogflow documentation lists Dialogflow ES separately from Conversational Agents, so do not assume ES is covered without confirmation. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa; https://cloud.google.com/vpc-service-controls/docs/supported-products; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/soc-2; https://docs.cloud.google.com/dialogflow/cx/docs |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts), 164.502(e) (Disclosures to Business Associates); NIST 800-53: SA-9 (External System Services), CM-8 (System Component Inventory), CM-7 (Least Functionality), RA-3 (Risk Assessment); HITRUST CSF v11: 05.k (Addressing Security in Third Party Agreements) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001210.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Compliance attestations relied upon for Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be retrieved, reviewed, and their exclusions tracked at least annually. |
| Rationale | An attestation is a point-in-time statement with a defined scope, and the useful information is usually in what it excludes rather than what it covers. For a suite whose components are certified individually and whose supporting services - Speech-to-Text, Cloud Storage, BigQuery, Cloud KMS, Pub/Sub - carry their own scope, the compliance picture is a composition rather than a single document. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Track the exclusions as a register rather than filing the reports. Google states that customers can request its SOC 2 reports through Compliance Reports Manager. Product names differ across attestations: at the time of authoring, Google's SOC 2 scope lists CX Agent Studio and CX Insights by name, while its HITRUST CSF scope list uses Conversational AI and Conversational Insights and does not name CX Agent Studio, so confirm with Google which entries cover each component. An exclusion that overlaps a control this guide depends on is a finding to escalate, not a footnote, and it will not announce itself between annual reviews. |
| References | https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/soc-2; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hitrust; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa; https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(8) (Evaluation), 164.308(b)(1) (Business Associate Contracts); NIST 800-53: CA-2 (Control Assessments), SA-9 (External System Services), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring), RA-3 (Risk Assessment); HITRUST CSF v11: 09.f (Monitoring and Review of Third Party Services) |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001220.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The complete data flow for member conversation content across all Gemini Enterprise for CX components and supporting services **MUST** be documented and maintained as certification evidence. |
| Rationale | Conversation content is written to at least six stores across two product APIs and five supporting Google Cloud services, each with independent encryption, residency, retention, and access configuration. Without a maintained data flow record, no one can answer where a given member's conversation exists, and every deletion, residency, and breach-scope question becomes an investigation. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Med |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  The record must name each store, the service that owns it, its encryption configuration, its region, its retention clock, and who can read it. Refresh it whenever a component, ingestion source, export, or subscriber is added, since each of those creates a new copy rather than a new view. Include transient stores the product exposes no configuration for, such as the session memcache and the Text-to-Speech cache, and every path by which content leaves the suite - exports, ingestion, tool calls, remote agents reached through the A2A Protocol, and third-party channel platforms. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/conversation-history; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/common-integrations; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/cmek |
| Mappings | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(1)(ii)(A) (Risk Analysis), 164.316(b)(1) (Documentation); NIST 800-53: PL-8 (Security and Privacy Architectures), CM-8 (System Component Inventory), RA-3 (Risk Assessment), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention); HITRUST CSF v11: 06.d (Data Protection and Privacy of Covered Information) |
| Detective Policies |  |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001230.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The residual risks this product cannot mitigate **MUST** be documented and formally accepted before production certification. |
| Rationale | Three exposures in this suite have no configuration remedy: tools execute with the project service agent's permissions rather than the caller's, data store retrieval is not documented as permission-aware, and per-conversation scoping in the analytics surface is available only as a Pre-GA feature. Each is compensated in this guide rather than closed, and a certification that does not name them implies a boundary that does not exist. |
| Estimated Security Value | High |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  State each residual risk with the compensating control that bounds it and the accepting authority. This is the record a later reviewer needs in order to distinguish a control that was considered and bounded from one that was overlooked. |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001240.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | Gemini Enterprise for CX agent applications **MUST** be registered in the organization's AI system inventory with a named accountable owner. |
| Rationale | A member-facing generative agent making statements about coverage and benefits is a consequential AI system under every governance framework the organization is likely to be measured against, and this suite makes creating one a low-friction action available to any holder of an editor role. An inventory is what makes the population of deployed agents knowable to the people accountable for them. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Register the agent, its purpose, its audience, its tool reach, and its accountable owner, and reconcile the inventory against the deployed agents on the quarterly cycle. The reconciliation is the control - an unregistered production agent is the finding it exists to surface. NIST AI 100-1 calls for mechanisms to inventory AI systems, resourced according to organizational risk priorities (GOVERN 1.6). |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/deploy; https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/NIST.AI.100-1.pdf |

| ID | SG-GCP-GECX-001250.0 |
| :--- | :--- |
| Requirement | The security posture of this deployment **MUST** be reassessed whenever a Gemini Enterprise for CX component transitions a capability from Preview to general availability. |
| Rationale | Several controls in this guide exist because a capability is Pre-GA, most consequentially the compensating access model that stands in for CX Insights fine-grained access control. When such a capability reaches GA the compensating control becomes unnecessary overhead and the stronger native control becomes available, so a guide that is never revisited keeps the organization on the weaker posture indefinitely. |
| Estimated Security Value | Med |
| Estimated Operational Impact | Low |
| Audit Procedures |  |
| Notes |  Review the CX Agent Studio, CX Insights, and Agent Assist release notes at least monthly to detect Preview-to-GA transitions. On a transition, revisit any prohibition on the capability, the compensating control that stood in for it, and the residual risk that was accepted because of it - all three change together. Increment the Revision column on each affected row rather than renumbering. |
| References | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/release-notes |

## Appendices

### Appendix A - List of Requirements

| ID | Requirement | Rationale | Estimated Security Value | Estimated Cost | Audit Procedure | Notes | References | Mappings |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000010 | Features of Gemini Enterprise for CX that are Non-GA or Pre-GA **MUST NOT** be used in production or with protected health information or other personal data. | Any capability not designated GA is governed by Google's Pre-GA Offerings Terms in the Service Specific Terms. Those terms state that Pre-GA offerings are provided "as is", may be changed, suspended, or discontinued without prior notice, are not covered by any SLA, and, unless Google documentation expressly says otherwise, are not covered by the Data Location section or by any data processing terms. They also state that "Customer should not use Pre-GA Offerings to process personal data or other data subject to legal or regulatory compliance requirements." Google's HIPAA guidance says separately: "Do not use Pre-GA offerings ... in connection with PHI". A Pre-GA capability handling member conversation content therefore sits outside the contractual perimeter the BAA establishes. The exposure is created by production reliance and by regulated data, so a Pre-GA feature exercised in an isolated non-production project on synthetic data processes nothing those terms leave unprotected. | High | Low |  | GA status is determined only from Google's release notes and product documentation with the Pre-GA Offerings Terms disclaimer removed; console availability, marketing pages, partner training material, and press announcements are not authoritative. Where sources disagree, the more restrictive stage governs, and a product-level GA announcement does not make GA the individual features it lists. The suite spans four documentation trees, three with independent release notes (CX Agent Studio, CX Insights, and Agent Assist), so confirm GA status per component and per feature rather than for the suite. Evaluation of a Pre-GA feature is permitted only in a non-production project that holds synthetic or otherwise non-personal data, shares no tool target, data store, service agent grant, or network path with production, and on which no production process depends. Keep a register of Pre-GA features under evaluation and of those blocked, and re-verify both at every release-note review cycle. | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/cx-agent-studio/resources/release-notes; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/insights/release-notes; https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/agent-assist/release-notes; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(8) (Evaluation); NIST 800-53: SA-8 (Security and Privacy Engineering Principles), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CM-7 (Least Functionality), SA-4 (Acquisition Process) |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000020 | Commerce agents **MUST NOT** be deployed. | Google's own documentation states that Commerce agents are not yet released: the product page ends "Commerce agents coming soon." An unreleased product has no published security documentation, no encryption or residency configuration surface, and no IAM model to assess. If it is first released as a preview, it will fall under the Pre-GA Offerings Terms and under Google's HIPAA guidance: "Do not use Pre-GA offerings ... in connection with PHI". The umbrella covered-products entry for Gemini Enterprise for Customer Experience does not by itself establish that a component with no GA release can be used with PHI. The intended capability set compounds this: Google describes Commerce agents as executing consented actions on a customer's behalf, which is a write-capable autonomous surface reaching commerce and order systems. | High | Low |  | This prohibition is a specific application of this guide's baseline prohibition on Non-GA and Pre-GA features, and stands until Commerce agents reach GA, are confirmed by Google as within the BAA-covered scope of Gemini Enterprise for CX, and are assessed under a fresh capability certification. Track the product page and the CX Agent Studio release notes for the transition. Nothing in this guide anticipates the control surface Commerce agents will present. | https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx/commerce-agents; https://docs.cloud.google.com/gemini-enterprise-cx; https://cloud.google.com/terms/service-terms; https://cloud.google.com/security/compliance/hipaa | HIPAA: 164.308(a)(8) (Evaluation); NIST 800-53: CM-7 (Least Functionality), SA-4 (Acquisition Process), SA-8 (Security and Privacy Engineering Principles), RA-3 (Risk Assessment) |

A beta or alpha version is conventionally a pre-release surface, so this guide treats both as Pre-GA as a matter of organizational policy until Google states their launch stage in writing; the policy does not assert that either version is outside the BAA, it removes production dependence on an unconfirmed answer. Because those tools execute with the identities configured on them - the project service agent where Service agent ID token authentication is used - rather than the end user's, a delegation chain does not narrow authority at each hop: the invoked agent's tools reach what their identities reach, regardless of what the caller was entitled to. A chain therefore aggregates rather than attenuates reach, and reviewing either agent alone will not reveal it. The A2A Protocol extends the same chain across application and project boundaries: a remote CX Agent Studio application is reached through the calling project's service agent, and an inbound A2A caller can write agent context directly into the invoked session. | High | Med |  | Record the full graph of which agents invoke which, and review the union of tools reachable through the graph rather than the tool list of any single agent. For the service agent's permissions, the project boundary is the only boundary this product offers. | High | High |  | This decision is made before the first agent application exists and is expensive to reverse, because moving an agent means recreating it, its tools, its deployment channels, and its data stores in a new project with a new perimeter. The sandbox identity, and whether its HTTP client attaches a Google credential, is undocumented; confirm with Google before using this tool type with member data, and never place a secret in the code, which is stored in the tool configuration. Google's pages disagree twice (the tool page cites the requests library while the runtime reference uses the provided client; the REST resource carries a Service Directory field although the tool page says private network access is unavailable), so verify behaviour in a non-production project. | SG-GCP-GECX-001310 | Tool invocations **MUST** carry a correlation identifier, comprising at minimum the agent application identifier and the conversation or session identifier, that the receiving backend system records in its own logs. | Tool calls commonly reach the backend under a shared platform or tool identity - the CX Agent Studio service agent, or a service account, OAuth client, or API key configured on the tool - so the backend's own audit log records one identity for many agents, conversations, and members. A deletion obligation - from the organization's records-retention schedule, from applicable state law, or from the business associate agreement's return-or-destroy clause on termination - that the organization cannot execute completely is a compliance failure regardless of intent. | High | Med |  | Build the procedure against the store inventory rather than against the console, and test it end to end with a synthetic conversation, verifying absence in every store afterwards. | SG-GCP-GECX-000010 | Features of Gemini Enterprise for CX that are Non-GA or Pre-GA **MUST NOT** be used in production or with protected health information or other personal data. |  |  |  |  |

| SG-GCP-GECX-000030 | The `v1beta` CX Agent Studio API and the `v1alpha1` CX Insights API **MUST NOT** be called from production workloads. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000040 | The Google Maps tool in CX Agent Studio **MUST NOT** be enabled on any agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000050 | CX Insights capabilities documented as Preview, including fine-grained access control, conversation datasets, and multiple scorecards, **MUST NOT** be used as the control of record or as a production dependency. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000060 | A capability certification review **MUST** be completed and approved before any Gemini Enterprise for CX component processes production member conversation data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000070 | Newly released Gemini Enterprise for CX features **MUST** be reviewed and explicitly approved before enablement, on a review cadence no longer than monthly. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000080 | The configuration baseline for every Gemini Enterprise for CX project - guardrails, tools, deployment channels, encryption, residency, retention, and audit settings - **MUST** be documented and drift-checked at least quarterly. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000090 | Personnel who build, deploy, or administer Gemini Enterprise for CX agent applications **SHOULD** complete role-specific training covering the confused-deputy tool execution model and the irreversible configuration decisions documented in this guide. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000100 | Administrative and builder access to CX Agent Studio, CX Insights, and Agent Assist **MUST** be federated through the organization's enterprise identity provider. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000110 | Multi-factor authentication **MUST** be enforced for every human user account holding any CX Agent Studio (`roles/ces.*`) or CX Insights (`roles/contactcenterinsights.*`) role. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000120 | The `roles/ces.admin` role **MUST** be granted only to named individual administrators. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000130 | Basic IAM roles - the legacy Owner, Editor, and Viewer roles and the Admin, Writer, and Reader basic roles - **MUST NOT** be granted on any project hosting a Gemini Enterprise for CX component. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000140 | The `roles/ces.securitySettingsEditor` role **MUST** be held by a different set of individuals than those holding `roles/ces.agentEditor` or `roles/ces.toolsEditor`. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000150 | The `roles/ces.deploymentEditor` role **MUST** be restricted to personnel explicitly authorized to promote changes to production. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000160 | Direct bindings of the `roles/ces.client` role **MUST** be limited to the service identities used by approved deployment channels and to the documented end-user principal set of an approved OAuth2 deployment channel. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000170 | The CX Agent Studio service agent `service-PROJECT_NUMBER@gcp-sa-ces.iam.gserviceaccount.com` **MUST** be granted only the minimum IAM roles required by the approved tools in that project. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000180 | Service account keys **MUST NOT** be created for any identity holding a Gemini Enterprise for CX role. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000190 | Entitlements to all `ces.googleapis.com` and `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` roles **MUST** be recertified at least quarterly by the accountable business owner. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000200 | Contact centre representatives receiving Agent Assist suggestions **MUST** authenticate through the organization's identity provider. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000210 | Standing administrative access to production Gemini Enterprise for CX projects **SHOULD** be replaced by time-bound, approved elevation. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000220 | Access to Gemini Enterprise for CX administrative surfaces **SHOULD** be conditioned on managed-device posture. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001410 | The `roles/ces.appEditor` role **SHOULD** be held by a different set of individuals than those holding `roles/ces.agentEditor` or `roles/ces.toolsEditor` in projects serving production traffic. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000230 | Guardrails **MUST** be explicitly configured and documented on every agent application rather than left at the shipped default. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000240 | The Safety guardrail **MUST** be set to Strict on every member-facing agent application, and to Strict or Balanced on every other agent application that can reach member data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000250 | The Prompt guard **MUST** be enabled on every agent application, with a defined outcome for a triggered guard. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000260 | A Blocklist guardrail **SHOULD** be configured on member-facing agent applications to suppress terms the organization has designated as prohibited in a generated response. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000270 | Agent instructions, prompts, application variables, and tool configurations **MUST NOT** contain credentials, API keys, tokens, or connection strings. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000280 | Every agent application **MUST** be security reviewed and approved before it is deployed to a channel carrying production traffic. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000290 | Agent evaluations **MUST** be executed and reviewed against a documented test set before an agent application is promoted to production, and after any change to its instructions, tools, or guardrails. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000300 | Agent applications **MUST NOT** be the sole authority for a coverage, eligibility, clinical, or other adverse determination communicated to a member. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000310 | Members interacting with a Gemini Enterprise for CX agent **MUST** be informed that they are interacting with an automated agent and offered a path to reach a human. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000320 | Agent applications generated by the Start with AI capability **MUST** be reviewed in full, including their generated instructions and tools, before deployment. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000330 | Where one agent is invoked as a tool of another - within one agent application or, through the A2A Protocol, across agent applications and projects - the delegation chain **MUST** be documented and reviewed as a single authorization surface. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000340 | Agent versioning and changelog history **SHOULD** be retained for the life of the agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001260 | Only models on the organization's approved model allowlist **MUST** be selected to power a Gemini Enterprise for CX agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001270 | A model designated Preview in the CX Agent Studio model picker **MUST NOT** be selected for an agent application carrying production traffic. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001280 | A model override applied to an individual sub-agent **MUST** be documented and approved as a change to the agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001290 | The launch stage of a selected model **MUST** be verified in the CX Agent Studio interface rather than inferred from that model's status in other Google Cloud products. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000350 | Each Gemini Enterprise for CX project **MUST** contain only agent applications that share a single trust domain. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000360 | An inventory of every registered tool, its type, its target system, its authentication method, and its approving owner **MUST** be maintained and reviewed at least quarterly. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000370 | Static credentials used by OpenAPI and MCP tools - API keys and any other secret held in the tool configuration - **MUST** be stored in Secret Manager and referenced rather than embedded in tool configuration, agent instructions, or custom header values. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000380 | A tool **MUST NOT** be able to retrieve data beyond what the principal conversing with the agent is authorized to see, unless the backend authorizes every request against an identity bound to the session at authentication rather than supplied by the model. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000390 | Data store tools and File search tools **MUST NOT** be grounded on content whose authorized audience is narrower than the audience able to converse with the agent. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000400 | Content ingested into a data store or RAG knowledge base used by a member-facing agent **MUST** be reviewed and approved before each ingestion and ingested only from locations under the organization's control. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000410 | Tool responses, retrieved data store content, and knowledge base excerpts **MUST** be treated as untrusted input that cannot authorize a subsequent action the caller could not authorize directly. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000420 | Third-party and externally hosted MCP servers **MUST** pass a supply chain security review before being registered as a tool. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000430 | MCP servers used by production agent applications **SHOULD** be hosted within the organization's Google Cloud boundary, with invocation restricted to the CX Agent Studio service agent. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000440 | Tools that connect to Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira, Confluence, or SharePoint - both the dedicated tool types for those systems and Integration Connector tools - **MUST** authenticate with the end user's own credentials or with a dedicated connection identity scoped to the minimum records the agent requires. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000450 | Python code tools **MUST** be reviewed as application source code before registration and before any subsequent modification. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000460 | Client function tools **MUST NOT** be passed data that the end user is not already authorized to see. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000470 | The Google Search tool **SHOULD NOT** be enabled on member-facing agent applications. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000480 | Where a tool's backend is reachable through Service Directory private network access, OpenAPI tools, MCP tools, and the dedicated Salesforce, ServiceNow, Jira, Confluence, and SharePoint tools **MUST** be configured to use the Service Directory path rather than a public endpoint. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000490 | The set of tools registered in a project **MUST** be restricted to those the approved agent use cases require, with unused registrations removed. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001310 | Tool invocations **MUST** carry a correlation identifier, comprising at minimum the agent application identifier and the conversation or session identifier, that the receiving backend system records in its own logs. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001330 | Results returned by a client function tool **MUST NOT** be relied upon to establish the end user's identity, entitlement, or authorization for any subsequent action. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001340 | Data store tools that reference a search engine **MUST** name the specific data stores to be searched rather than leaving the data store selection empty. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001360 | The RAG knowledge base behind a File search tool **MUST** be located in a US region where RAG Engine is designated GA. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001370 | RAG knowledge bases used by File search tools **MUST** be protected by customer-managed encryption keys and placed inside the VPC Service Controls perimeter before any file is uploaded. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001380 | File search tools **SHOULD NOT** be used to ground agents on content containing protected health information. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001390 | Agent-to-agent (A2A) Protocol tools **MUST NOT** be configured on any agent application, for outbound delegation or for inbound invocation, while the capability is designated Preview. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000500 | An inventory of every deployment channel, its authentication mode, and its approving owner **MUST** be maintained for each agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000510 | Public access through the Google-hosted token broker **MUST NOT** be enabled for any agent application whose tools can reach member data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000520 | Where public access is approved for a bounded public-information agent, origin checks and reCAPTCHA **MUST** both be enabled on the widget configuration. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000530 | Agent applications serving authenticated members **MUST** authenticate each member individually through the organization's identity provider - using the OAuth2 client path or a custom authentication API that issues a short-lived token for the member's session - and grant member identities no role that includes the `ces.tools.execute` permission. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000540 | A self-hosted token broker **SHOULD** be used in preference to the Google-hosted broker for any agent application that serves unauthenticated callers or bounded public information. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000550 | The web widget **MUST** be embedded only on web origins the organization controls. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000560 | Non-production agent development and testing **MUST** be performed in a separate Google Cloud project from production, using deployment environments that carry no production traffic. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000570 | Traffic splitting **MUST NOT** direct production conversations to an agent version that has not completed security review and evaluation. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000580 | Third-party platforms that carry member conversations to or from a deployment channel - contact center providers such as AudioCodes, Five9, SecureCo, and Twilio, messaging platforms such as WhatsApp and Instagram, and any comparable intermediary - **MUST** have completed a third-party security assessment and, where they process ePHI, be covered by an executed business associate agreement. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000590 | Direct API access to deployed agent applications **MUST** be routed through the organization's API gateway rather than exposed to callers as a direct Google endpoint. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000600 | Voice channels **MUST** present a recording and automated-interaction disclosure to the member before conversation content is captured. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001300 | Quota caps and cost alerting **MUST** be configured for every agent application exposed on a public or high-volume deployment channel. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001400 | WhatsApp and Instagram deployments **MUST NOT** be created for any Gemini Enterprise for CX agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000610 | Customer-managed encryption keys **MUST** be initialized for Agent Assist before it processes any production conversation. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000620 | Sensitive Data Protection redaction **MUST** be configured for Agent Assist conversation data using an inspect template and a deidentify template. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000630 | Agent Assist conversation data retention **MUST** be configured to the shortest period that meets a documented business need. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000640 | Generated conversation summaries **MUST** be reviewed by the human representative before they are committed to a system of record. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000650 | Smart Reply, AI coach, and other generated response suggestions **MUST NOT** be transmitted to a member without an affirmative action by the human representative. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000660 | Knowledge bases used by Knowledge Assist and Generative Knowledge Assist **MUST** be scoped to content the entire representative population is authorized to see. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000670 | Live translation output **MUST NOT** be relied upon as the authoritative record of a member interaction. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000680 | Agent Assist deployments **MUST** be pinned to a region consistent with the organization's data residency position, with per-feature residency coverage verified before enablement. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000690 | Where Agent Assist data about identified human representatives - for example, AI coach suggestion usage or conversations passed to CX Insights for Quality AI scoring - is used to evaluate representative performance, that evaluation **MUST** be governed by the organization's employee monitoring and workforce policy before enablement. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001320 | The Agent Assist Build your own assist capability **MUST NOT** be enabled while it is designated Preview. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001350 | Gemini Transcribe Live **MUST NOT** be enabled in any Agent Assist conversation profile while it is designated Preview. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000700 | The CX Insights encryption specification **MUST** be initialized with a customer-managed key for every location before any conversation is ingested into that location. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000710 | A project-level `conversation_ttl` **MUST** be configured in CX Insights before the first conversation is ingested. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000720 | Sensitive Data Protection redaction **MUST** be applied on every CX Insights ingestion path before production conversations are imported - through RedactionConfig for upload and ingest paths, and in the upstream Agent Assist or CX Agent Studio configuration for runtime integrations. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000730 | Project-level CX Insights access - `roles/contactcenterinsights.viewer`, `roles/contactcenterinsights.editor`, and `roles/contactcenterinsights.admin` granted directly, and the same access inherited through any CX Agent Studio role (`roles/ces.*`) - **MUST** be held only by a named, minimal, documented group. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000740 | CX Insights authorized views **MUST NOT** be applied to conversation data containing PHI while the capability is Pre-GA, or configured with an empty filter expression in any project. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000750 | The CX Insights global endpoint **MUST NOT** be used for production conversation data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000760 | Cloud Storage buckets holding conversation audio for CX Insights **MUST** be encrypted with customer-managed keys, located in the approved region, and placed inside the service perimeter. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000770 | Cloud Speech-to-Text **MUST** be configured with customer-managed encryption keys where it transcribes conversation audio for CX Insights or Agent Assist. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000780 | BigQuery datasets receiving the CX Insights conversation export **MUST** be encrypted with customer-managed keys, located in the approved region, and placed inside the service perimeter. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000790 | CX Insights Pub/Sub notifications for triggers whose message data is a Conversation resource - create-conversation and update-conversation, including when all events are selected - **MUST NOT** be routed to a topic whose subscribers are not approved to receive member conversation content. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000800 | The CX Insights Google Sheets integration **MUST NOT** be used in any project that holds member conversation data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000810 | Topic models, scorecards, summarization generators, and other artifacts trained or derived from conversation content **MUST** be treated as carrying the sensitivity of the source conversations. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000820 | Where Quality AI assessments evaluate identified human representatives, their use **MUST** be approved under the organization's workforce monitoring policy before enablement. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000830 | Conversations ingested into CX Insights during pilot or proof-of-concept activity **MUST** be deleted before the production deployment is certified, unless they were ingested under the full production control set. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000840 | CX Agent Studio **MUST** be CMEK-integrated at project creation, before any agent application or conversation data exists in the project. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000850 | Cloud KMS keys protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX data **MUST** be monitored for availability, with key destruction and disablement gated on multi-party authorization. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000860 | Cloud KMS key versions protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX data **MUST NOT** be disabled or destroyed while any data encrypted under them is still retained. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000870 | CX Agent Studio agent data **MUST** be located in the `us` multi-region. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000880 | Cloud Logging **MUST** be configured with a US region for log storage independently of the Gemini Enterprise for CX residency setting. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000890 | Conversation history retention in CX Agent Studio **MUST** be configured to a documented period no longer than the business need requires. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000900 | The state of the Log your customer conversations setting (labeled Interaction data in agent application settings) **MUST** be an explicit, documented decision for every agent application. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000910 | Written confirmation from Google that the Service Specific Terms training restriction covers every Gemini Enterprise for CX component and data path in use **MUST** be obtained and retained as certification evidence. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000920 | Sensitive Data Protection templates used by any Gemini Enterprise for CX component **MUST** be located inside the same VPC Service Controls perimeter as the component that references them. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000930 | Member identifiers and other sensitive values **MUST NOT** be written into application variables, agent instructions, or tool configuration as static values. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000940 | A documented procedure **MUST** exist to locate and delete all copies of an individual member's conversation data across every store in the deployment. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000950 | The short-lived caches the product maintains **MUST** be documented in the data flow record supporting certification. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000960 | Google personnel access to Gemini Enterprise for CX conversation data **SHOULD** be gated by Access Approval and recorded through Access Transparency. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000970 | A VPC Service Controls perimeter **MUST** be configured with `ces.googleapis.com`, `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com`, and, where Agent Assist is used, `dialogflow.googleapis.com` in the restricted services list. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000980 | The service perimeter **MUST** be established and enforced before agent applications, data stores, deployment channels, and CX Insights locations are created. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-000990 | The service perimeter **MUST** be validated in dry-run mode and its findings resolved before enforcement. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001000 | Tools and callbacks **MUST NOT** be able to call HTTP(S) endpoints outside the approved list, enforced through the CX Agent Studio allowed origins endpoint control policy with its enforcement scope set to ALWAYS. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001010 | Service Directory private network access **MUST** be configured for OpenAPI tool targets hosted inside the organization's VPC network. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001020 | Cloud Storage buckets, BigQuery datasets, Secret Manager secrets, Sensitive Data Protection templates, and data stores referenced by Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** reside inside the same service perimeter as the components that reference them. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001030 | Every ingress rule, egress rule, and allowed origin for the Gemini Enterprise for CX perimeter **MUST** name specific identities, sources, services, or URLs - never ANY_IDENTITY, an access level or service name of "*" - and carry a documented owner and a review at least annually. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001040 | Flow-based agents **MUST NOT** reference agent resources located outside the service perimeter. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001050 | Import and export operations for agent configuration **MUST** target Cloud Storage buckets inside the service perimeter. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001060 | Changes to the service perimeter protecting Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be change controlled and alerted on. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001070 | Data Access audit logs for `ces.googleapis.com` **MUST** be explicitly enabled for all three sub-types: ADMIN_READ, DATA_READ, and DATA_WRITE. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001080 | Data Access audit logs for `contactcenterinsights.googleapis.com` **MUST** be explicitly enabled for all three sub-types: ADMIN_READ, DATA_READ, and DATA_WRITE. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001090 | Audit logs from every Gemini Enterprise for CX project **MUST** be exported to the organization's security information and event management platform. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001100 | Audit log retention **MUST** meet or exceed the organization's retention requirement for records of access to protected health information. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001110 | Exported audit logs **MUST** be protected against modification and deletion by the administrators of the Gemini Enterprise for CX projects. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001120 | Alerts **MUST** be configured for changes to CX Agent Studio security settings and to agent application logging, redaction, and export settings. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001130 | Alerts **MUST** be configured for conversation deletion and bulk deletion operations in CX Agent Studio and CX Insights. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001140 | Alerts **MUST** be configured for tool registration and modification in CX Agent Studio and for changes to the tools assigned to agents. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001150 | Alerts **MUST** be configured for anomalous read volume against CX Insights conversation data. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001160 | Operational monitoring **MUST NOT** depend solely on the product's built-in conversation dashboards where conversation logging is disabled or can be disabled. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001170 | An incident response procedure covering Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be documented, including containment steps for a compromised agent application or tool. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001180 | A business associate agreement covering Google Cloud **MUST** be executed and its scope confirmed to include every Gemini Enterprise for CX component in use before production member conversation data is processed. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001190 | Gemini Enterprise for CX components **MUST NOT** process protected health information unless Google's HIPAA covered products list names the component or Google confirms in writing that the component falls within a listed product. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001200 | Every Google API in the path of member conversation data, including the Dialogflow API (`dialogflow.googleapis.com`) used by Agent Assist and Conversational Agents, **MUST** be confirmed by Google in writing as within a product on Google's HIPAA covered products list before it processes protected health information. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001210 | Compliance attestations relied upon for Gemini Enterprise for CX **MUST** be retrieved, reviewed, and their exclusions tracked at least annually. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001220 | The complete data flow for member conversation content across all Gemini Enterprise for CX components and supporting services **MUST** be documented and maintained as certification evidence. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001230 | The residual risks this product cannot mitigate **MUST** be documented and formally accepted before production certification. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001240 | Gemini Enterprise for CX agent applications **MUST** be registered in the organization's AI system inventory with a named accountable owner. |  |  |  |  |
| SG-GCP-GECX-001250 | The security posture of this deployment **MUST** be reassessed whenever a Gemini Enterprise for CX component transitions a capability from Preview to general availability. |  |  |  |  |
